Format (debug-form elements/keys/values; `Str` quoted+escaped): List `"[a, b, c]"` (empty `"[]"`); Option `"Some(x)"` / `"None"` (None bare, no parens); Map `"{k: v}"` (empty `"{}"`), `, ` between elements/entries, `: ` between map key and value, no inner padding. A non-Debug element/key/value type is rejected with **`E0520`** naming the offending inner type (`Map` requires both `K` and `V`); **no placeholder** for this case (would relocate the banned silent fallback one level down). **One container level for v1**: a nested container (`List[List[T]]`, `Map[K, List[V]]`, …) is a **hard `E0520` at depth+1, never a placeholder** (message names the v1 limit + tracking ticket). Soundness lives in the inductive typecheck rule (recurses to prove every nested element is `Debug`); the fully-recursive per-monomorphization `debug()` emitter is the deferred end-state (follow-up ticket; mirror the `blink_promote_{mangle}` descriptor emitter). §3.6 trait table + new *Container Debug Rendering* subsection | 6-0 Q1 implement Debug / drop `<?>`; 6-0 Q2 format (independent convergence); 6-0 Q3 reject non-Debug element via extended E0520; 3-3 R1 → **5-1 R2** Q4 one-level (sys dissent → full; web/aiml flipped after Phase D); AI-First 0/5 fail |
| `Char` debug-form | `Char.debug()` = the character in **single quotes**, escaping exactly the ratified `Char` literal set (`\n \r \t \\ \b \f \0 \'`); every other scalar (printable ASCII + all non-ASCII) emitted as its literal UTF-8 char between the quotes. `'a'`→`'a'`, `'\n'`→`'\n'`, `'\''`→`'\''`, `'😀'`→`'😀'`. **Changes the current `97`** (bare code point — an accidental fall-through of `Char` into the `is_hash_scalar_ct → int_to_str` numeric bucket; never a designed contract). Rationale is injectivity/faithfulness: `'a'`→`97` collapses `Char 'a'` onto `Int 97` (and would make `Map[Char,Int]{'a':1}` byte-identical to `Map[Int,Int]{97:1}`), violating the 5-0 faithfulness ban and silently doing the explicit-only `Char→Int` coercion; single-quoting also keeps `Char` distinct from a 1-char `Str` (`'a'`≠`"a"`) and consistent with "`Char` is not numeric" (§3c). Invariant: `Char.debug()` emits only escapes the lexer accepts, so `parse(c.debug()) == c` for every `c` in the ratified set (round-trip). **Control-char `'\u{N}'` output deferred-and-linked to the hex-escape task** (adds `\u{...}` as input syntax): until then a non-printable scalar with no named escape (e.g. `U+0007`) is emitted as its raw byte(s) — faithful, not always legible. Flows unchanged into `Char` struct fields / `List[Char]` / `Option[Char]` / `Map[Char,V]` keys via one shared helper (they agree by construction). §3.6 Debug table + *Debug vs Display* scalar debug-forms paragraph | 6-0 Q1 single-quoted (Option A over keep-`97` / double-quote-as-Str / bare-char); 6-0 Q2 raw-glyph for exotic controls, `\u{N}` deferred to that task (plt conceded A-hex→A-raw); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Unicode escapes (`\u{H}`) and `Char`/`Str` debug output | **`\u{H}`** (1–6 hex digits, either case) is valid in `"..."` and `'...'`, not in `#"..."#`; the value must be a Unicode scalar value (same bounds as `Char.from_code_point`); the `{` after `\u` is part of the escape, never an interpolation hole; `"\u{0}"` is an error (Str cannot hold NUL). No other numeric escape: `\x..`, `\uXXXX`, `\U...` and each malformed `\u` are lexer errors in both literal kinds, each with its own registered E11xx code (via `diag_error_range`, span on the escape, `\u{..}` fix-it; no new uncoded sites). **One shared escape table**: common `\n \r \t \\ \b \f \u{H}`; Char adds `\0 \'`; Str adds `\" \{ \}` (Str table now lists `\b \f`, which the lexer already accepted). **`Char.debug()` and `Str.debug()` share one rule**: named escape → `\u{h}` (lowercase, no leading zeros) for the escape class → raw UTF-8. Escape class = Cc plus a **frozen** list U+200B–200F, 2028–2029, 202A–202E, 2060–2064, 2066–2069, FEFF; changes only by a new spec decision, never by a Unicode update. Round-trip `parse(x.debug()) == x` for every Char and Str. Raw UTF-8 char literals (`'😀'`) stay a separate lexer bug that blocks the implementation. `blink fmt` keeps the author's hex spelling. Closes the deferral in `Char` debug-form | 6-0 Q1 scope; 6-0 Q2 escape class; 5-1 Q3 raw-UTF-8 fix as blocker (AI/ML: same change); 6-0 Q4 Str errors; 5-1 Q5 fmt keeps spelling (DevOps: canonicalize hex); 6-0 Q6 shared table; Q7 coded diagnostics 3-3 → 6-0 in Phase D |
| FFI import namespace resolution | `ffi` stays a no-import intrinsic namespace (like `io`/`net`/`time`); `import blink.ffi`/`import blink.core` resolve as **recognized, inert, optional no-ops** — never required, never `ModuleNotFound`. Selective names (`Ptr`/`Void`/`alloc_ptr`/`null_ptr`) are satisfiable no-ops. **No E/W import gate**: the unsafe surface is gated only by `PtrOutsideFFI` (E0811 — `Ptr[T]` inside `@ffi`/`@trusted`) and `#275` (`@ffi` requires a `[native-dependencies]` manifest entry). Non-breaking; no lib/std or test migration. (2026-07-07) | 6-0 |
| Enums nominally distinct from `Int` | Delete the enum↔Int assignability carve-out in `types_compatible`: an enum is **not** assignable to/from `Int` at let-binding, argument, or return (falls to `ka == kb` → 0, mirroring the sized-int rule). **Comparison stays legal** (`==`/`<`… short-circuit to `Bool` before `types_compatible`, comparing the shared tag). Explicit conversion only: `Enum.to_int()` (total) / `Enum.from_int(n) -> Option[Enum]` (fallible); **no `as` cast operator** added. Migration is a **hard flip** (no deprecation phase) — the 16 affected sites are all compiler-internal and their fixes (`new_node(kind: NodeKind)`, `Token.kind: TokenKind`, `peek_kind` return, `_last_kind` sentinel) advance the planned `kind:Int → NodeKind` migration. **Match-scrutinee** enforcement (`match someInt { Variant => }` ill-typed) is part of the rule but its enforcement is **staged** behind the `kind:Int → NodeKind` migration. `Errno(Int)` stays an enum, distinct for free. Out of scope: struct-literal field values are unchecked. §3.4 *Enums Are Nominally Distinct from `Int`* | 6-0 Q1 delete carve-out (Option A); 5-1 Q2 stage match-scrutinee (PLT dissent → NOW, feasibility-conceded); 6-0 Q3 Errno stays enum; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Under-determined types are a hard error (no silent default, no surface `unknown`) | Inference is **two-state**: every type variable resolves to a concrete type, or the unresolved ones are **reported** — never defaulted. When Hindley-Milner finishes a binding with a type variable still unbound (not fixed by annotation or by later use), that binding is **`error[CannotInferType]` (E0301)**, applied uniformly to empty `[]`, bare `None`, empty `Map()`/`Set()`, and under-constrained generic construction (`GKV { m: Map() }`) — one rule, one diagnostic. **No surface `unknown`/`any`/`?` type**: `TK_UNKNOWN` stays a transient internal inference state, never nameable/holdable/inferred into a program (TS `unknown` is opted-into, never *produced* by inference; a top type in a monomorphizing backend forces boxing/RTTI). **Strict, no unobserved-local carve-out**: `let x = []; x.len()` errors (an element-agnostic use does not constrain the element); making legality depend on whether the value is later observed needs a whole-function escape analysis that breaks locality of reasoning and is refactor-fragile. **Report-at-binding, dual-span**: primary caret + machine-applicable annotation fix at the `let` (`: List[T]` / `: Map[K,V]` / `: T?`, ctor shape known), secondary "blame" span at the empty ctor (rejects OCaml weak-var deferred-to-boundary reporting — fires far from source, no single fix). **ICE backstop `UnsolvedTypeVarAtCodegen` (I0001)**: an unsolved `TK_TYPEVAR`/`TK_UNKNOWN` reaching `tc_tid_to_c_tag`/`type_name_from_ct` must fail closed, never fall through to `"Void"` (must key on the *kind*, not the resulting `"Void"` tag, so a genuine `GKV[Void,Void]` is unaffected). Pre-metavar accident (non-uniform: `Map()`→wrong `kops_str` vtable, `None`→arbitrary `blink_Option_int`, `GKV{m:Map()}`→`blink_GKV_Void_Void`+runtime `ensure_kops` patch). Migration: mechanical annotation (`blink fix`-able); sweep compiler's own sources before the flip (self-host). **Status: implemented** — E0301 + I0001 land the decision; the `ensure_kops` runtime patch is retired (ctor-time pinning is now the sole mechanism). **AMENDED by *Explicit type application* (below, V1-c, 6-0)**: report-at-binding is generalized to *report where the repair attaches* — for a type parameter the signature does not supply, that is the call's type-argument position, and "the single repair is a type annotation" is narrowed to bindings. §3.4 *Under-Determined Types*; catalog E0301 + I0001 | 6-0 Q1 error/never-default; 6-0 Q2 reject surface `unknown`; 6-0 Q3 strict no carve-out (plt+sys withdrew Phase-A carve-outs in debate); 6-0 Q4 report-at-binding dual-span; 6-0 Q5 ICE tripwire; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Explicit type application, and an undeterminable type parameter is a lint not an error | **Bracketed call-site type application is part of Blink** and §3.4 now defines it — a *third* supply mechanism beside inference at a construction site and an annotation on the binding (ratifies ~50 attestations across 8 spec files, incl. a diagnostic's own `help:` text and normative FFI signatures `alloc_ptr[T]() -> Ptr[T]`; §2.6's 5-0 bracket vote already foreclosed an index operator to pay for the slot). **No erasure**: every binder a declaration introduces stays in its monomorphization key — never dropped, defaulted, or collapsed onto another instantiation's C symbol (blocks the ODR collapse where `parse_or_zero[Int]()` and `parse_or_zero[Duration]()` share `() -> Int`). **Brackets are mandatory** at every call of a type parameter the signature does not supply; *supplied by the signature* = occurs in a parameter type or the return type, decidable from the signature alone with no call site and no body consulted. **E0301 is reported where its repair attaches** (amends *Under-determined types*) — the type-argument position for an unsupplied binder, including a call in statement position, which previously fell through to I0001. **Two repairs in a normative (not presentational) order**: delete the binder when the lint says it is removable, else supply the type argument — deletion named first, so an LSP one-click cannot launder the defect into working code. **One discipline for all type-argument lists**: all-or-none, exact arity, bounds checked, permitted on a struct-literal head (`Registry[User] { entries: [] }`). **A redundant type-argument list carries no diagnostic of any class** (a warning would be non-monotonic — a later annotation elsewhere would retroactively make unchanged code noisy). **Phantom type parameters are legal in user code** (`type Template[C]`'s 4-1 is not reserved to compiler-known types). **No new declaration-site error**: a `fn`/`type` binder that occurs nowhere gets **`W0604 UnusedTypeParamBinder`**, because `∀a. Int` has the witness `f[Int]()` and a type system may not reject a declaration some well-typed use inhabits. The **normative gate is the occurrence clause** — *`T` occurs nowhere in the declaration or its body* — with fix-safety ("W0604 fires exactly when deleting the binder is a safe edit") adopted as **normative rationale** beside it; **a bound is an occurrence**, so `fn assert_serializable[T: Serialize]() {}` is a compile-time assertion and never warns. **An impl binder is still rejected outright (E0909)** and a `where`-clause bound does not rescue it — reconciled by: reject a declaration only when *no* use site could ever repair it; where one can, the diagnostic goes to the use site and the declaration gets a lint. E0909's shipped explain text ("no call site could ever determine what it stands for") is **deleted** as the false generalization this ticket was filed on. New §3.1 ***Diagnostic Discipline* (normative)**: never emit a diagnostic whose prescribed repair does not exist; no rule enforced only at codegen (I0001 catches violations of that rule, it is never one); co-firing diagnostics must be confluent and the converging repair must be named first. §3.1 *Diagnostic Discipline*, §3.4 *Explicit Type Application*, §3.6 impl-binder rule; catalog E0301 (spec-ref corrected §3.3→§3.4) + new W0604 | 6-0 V1 ratify+no-erasure; 6-0 V1-b normative fix order; 6-0 V1-c report-where-repair-attaches; 6-0 V2 bound-is-an-occurrence; **6-0 R2 V3** occurrence clause as gate / fix-safety as rationale (after 4-1-1 R1 — all four fix-safety voters moved, each off a reason it had authored); 5-1 V4 brackets on a struct-literal head (aiml: one spelling per site); 6-0 V5 no diagnostic on a redundant list; 6-0 V6 bound does not rescue an impl binder; 6-0 V7(i) delete the E0909 sentence / 5-1 V7(ii) keep `ImplBinderUnused` (aiml: rename to `ImplBinderUndeterminable`) / 6-0 V7(iii) E0909 stays; 5-1 V8 "no diagnostic whose repair does not exist" (sys: route to the catalog preamble); 6-0 V9 T1 + confluence; 6-0 V10 code allocation; R5-a declaration-site error does not land (4 NO, 2 YES conditional on an antecedent that lost 6-0) |
| `fs` operations return `Result[T, FsError]`, not a bare `Str` | The four v1 filesystem operations are `fs.read(path) -> Result[Str, FsError] ! FS.Read`, `fs.write(path, content) -> Result[Void, FsError] ! FS.Write`, `fs.list_dir(path) -> Result[List[Str], FsError] ! FS.Read`, `fs.delete(path) -> Result[Void, FsError] ! FS.Delete`. **Error type is a per-domain rich `FsError { op: FsOp, path: Str, code: Errno }`** with `FsOp { Read Write ListDir Delete }` — matching the `net`→`NetError` / `db`→`DbError` convention, *not* a bare `Errno` (the stdlib already runs per-domain errors, so bare-Errno would be the special case; and a path is permanently unrecoverable once `?` propagates past the call frame — Rust's `fs_err` crate is the ecosystem receipt). `code: Errno` retained as the transparent zero-cost OS code (A1: `libc.*` / `io.*` keep bare `Errno`; this does not add a general `IoError`). `FsError` **impl `Display`** (Go-style `op path: message`, a same-change blocker) and ships a `not_found()` predicate; `FsError` + `FsOp` are **prelude-promoted**. **Names follow the spec, not the impl**: `fs.list_dir` (Q3a — listing is `FS.Read`, there is no `FS.List`), `fs.delete` + `FS.Delete` (Q3b — matched verb/effect pair; the impl's `fs.remove` / `FS.Remove` rejected). **No file-handle API in v1** (Q2): exactly the four path ops, no `fs.open` / `fs.create` / `fs.read(handle)`; streaming/incremental access is post-v1 as *methods on a file handle* (`file.read_line()`), never an `fs.read` overload (Blink has no function overloading). `?` is exact (no implicit `.into()`); cross-error adaptation uses `.map_err`. **Riders**: W0603 `UnusedResult` lint same-change (`let _ =` opt-out) *(erratum: W0603 is `ShadowedVariable` in the spec and the compiler, so the voted `UnusedResult` lint has code W0605, reserved in `ERROR_CATALOG.md` and not yet emitted)*; `map_err` is a v1-blocking dependency (spec'd, currently unimplemented); user-facing named errno constants deferred, but the internal errno→message+NAME table ships as `Display`'s data source. **Staged implementation** (user directive): fix the silent miscompile (`Errno` struct field constructed flat into a boxed field) + the whole-value `Err(e)` binder on `Result[T, Errno]`) → the C2 "box struct error arms by pointer" codegen rule (guarded by a `Result[Bytes, Errno]` transparent-emit pin-test) → `map_err` → the `FsError` fs-intrinsics. §4.4.5 *Filesystem Operations* | Q1 6-0 (`FsError`, after 3-3 R1 → 6-0 R2); Q2 6-0 (no handle API); Q3a 6-0 (`fs.list_dir`); Q3b 5-1 (web dissent: `fs.remove`); riders Ra/Rb/Rd 6-0 same-change, Re/Rf tracked; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| An under-determined sum-variant type parameter is `error[CannotInferType]`, uniform across every position | Resolves the position-dependent split where `let r = Ok(3)` errored E0301 but `match (Ok(3), 9) { ... }` was silently accepted and codegen fabricated an arbitrary `blink_Result_int_str` carrier. **Q1 (6-0):** an unconstrained sum-variant type parameter — an Ok-only `Result` error type, a None-only `Option` inner — is a free metavariable α at a materialization boundary, the same case *Under-determined types* already resolved 6-0. It is **`error[CannotInferType]` (E0301), position-independent** — the judgment is a property of the type, not of where the value sits, so the same E0301 fires at a `let`, a tuple element, a match scrutinee, a struct field, a call argument, and a return. Delete the `codegen_types.bl` fabrication fallthroughs (`Result_int_str`, `Option_int`); an unsolved variable reaching monomorphization fails closed at **I0001**, which keys on the metavar **kind**, never on the resulting concrete tag (a tag-keyed check would mask a genuine `Result[Void, Str]` / user-written `Result[Int, Never]` and let a real fabrication slip through). **Reject Never/⊥ defaulting** — inferring `Never` into an unconstrained slot is the identical unlicensed `[⊥/α]` substitution *Under-determined types* banned as `[Void/α]`; a `Never` error type is legitimate only when a program *writes* `Result[Int, Never]`, never chosen by inference. **Q3 (4-2 → soft consensus):** the normative repair at non-`let` positions is in-place explicit type application `Ok[Int, Str](3)` (a constructor is a callee, entailed by *Explicit type application*); reported per the declaration-site rule (the binding when one dominates, else the constructor's type-argument position). **Impl gate:** the diagnostic emits that spelling as its machine-applicable fix only once parse+codegen support for variant-constructor type arguments lands (zero occurrences in-tree today); until then the emitted repair is lift-to-annotated-`let`. Correctness fix (delete the seam) ships regardless of the gate. Unblocks deleting the flat `ScopeVar` type fields. **Status: spec resolved; implementation tracked separately.** §3.4 *Under-Determined Types* | Q1 6-0 (E0301 uniform / delete fabrication / reject Never-default); Q3 4-2 (A: web, plt, devops, aiml · B: sys, min), Phase D skipped — soft consensus (the A-majority's Concern fields endorse the dissent's parse-support precondition, which the impl gate satisfies); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| A type expression must be applied to its exact arity (bare/under-/over-applied constructor is `error[TypeArgArity]`) | A type constructor of arity *n* in a **type position** (parameter, return, field, binding annotation, nested type argument, struct-literal head) denotes a complete type **only** when applied to exactly *n* type arguments; the wrong count — **including none** — is **`error[TypeArgArity]` (E0303)**. Reframes the ticket: the defect is **not** Channel-specific but the general absence of kind-checking on type expressions. **One code both directions** — under-application (too few, of which the bare name `Channel` / `List` / `Map` is the *k*=0 extreme) and over-application (`List[Int, Str]`) are one failure (applied arity ≠ declared arity), not three cases. **Uniform across builtins and user generics** — `Channel`/`List`/`Map`/`Set`/`Option` are constructors on the same terms as a user's `type Registry[T]`; a bare `Registry` is E0303 for the same reason a bare `Channel` is. **Decided at name resolution, before inference** — arity is a property of the declaration alone, so E0303 is **not** E0301: E0301 fires when inference *terminates* with a variable no use fixed; E0303 fires when a type expression was never well-formed, and no downstream use can fill an argument the annotation never opened. The two never co-fire on one type expression (ill-formed rejected first). **Case-specific normative repair (4-row)**: bare + type param in scope → apply it (`Channel[T]`); bare + none in scope → declare a binder on the enclosing decl and apply it (`fn relay[T](src: Channel[T], dst: Channel[T])`); under-applied → supply the missing argument; over-applied → remove the extra. Binder-declaring repair offered **only** when bare *and* no param in scope can fill the slot. **Trait references are outside the rule** (plt drafting guard): a trait name in a bound (`T: Ord`) or impl header is **not** a type position — it constrains a parameter rather than denoting a type, so `fn sort[T: Ord](xs: List[T])` is well-formed under E0303; trait-argument arity is `error[TraitArgArity]` (E0910), the impl-header specialization of the same principle (one concept, two codes, differing grammar/repair). **Rejects Option 2** (bare `Channel` = implicit `Channel[T]`): implicit quantification cannot express the shared-`T` `relay` needs and as specified merely renames the bug. **Rejects Option 3** (untyped escape hatch / status quo): violates the 6-0 under-determined-types decision and drives a real miscompile lane (element-known vs element-unknown channel wire formats). Migration is a two-step bootstrap (migrate lib/std bare `List`/`Map`/`Set` FFI externs first, then enable E0303). §3.4 *Kind-Correctness of Type Expressions*; catalog E0303 | Q1 6-0 reject bare/under-applied Channel; Q2 6-0 new dedicated `error[TypeArgArity]` E0303 at the annotation, one code both directions (sys withdrew reuse-E0301, min withdrew reuse-E0910); Q3 6-0 general kind-correctness rule over all builtins + user generics (web withdrew fallback); no Phase D (unanimous); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Opaque FFI pointers get an honest type (`@ffi.opaque`) | An opaque C handle — an **incomplete** C type on the Blink side (`sqlite3*`, `FILE*`, `SSL_CTX*`) that must also flow through ordinary non-FFI code — gets a **bare nominal, arity-0** type via `@ffi.opaque(header, name) type Foo`. Lowers to `name *`, one machine word; **no `sizeof`/`offsetof` `_Static_assert`** (incomplete by design — the deliberate contrast with `@ffi.struct`). **E0811-exempt by construction, not by carve-out** — an `@ffi.opaque` type is not a `Ptr[T]`, so `PtrOutsideFFI` containment stays purely syntactic and the handle flows through struct fields / wrapper fns with no `@trusted`. **Nominally distinct per declaration** (`Sqlite3` ≠ `Sqlite3Stmt`), fixing the `Ptr[Void]` collapse where every opaque handle was one type. **Inert** — no `.deref()`/`.addr()`/`.offset()`/field access/`==`; the value can only be held, passed, returned, stored (zero raw-pointer surface in non-FFI code). **Nullability = `Option[Foo]` only** (no `.is_null()`, no null sentinel, no `Opaque[T]?`), with **guaranteed null-pointer optimization** — the C `NULL` pointer *is* the `None` niche, so a nullable handle is one word and one compare, never a tagged struct. **Construction sealed to the FFI boundary** — a handle is produced only as an `@ffi` return or by dereferencing a `Ptr[Foo]` out-cell inside `@ffi`/`@trusted`; fabricating one from ordinary code is rejected by the existing construction / type-mismatch diagnostics. **Zero new error/warning codes** (reuses no-method + type-mismatch machinery); the sole new artifact is the `blink audit` category `opaque-ffi-handle`. Rejected: **B** `Opaque[Tag]` arity-1 generic (a type constructor with no functorial content — pays E0303/carrier-encoding/tid-mono-CSV/@derive seams for zero soundness gain, and `Opaque[Int]` invites the container-of-Bar misread); **C** narrow-E0811-for-`Ptr[Void]` (leaves the type collapsed, makes the default path the broken one, and leaks `.addr()`/`==` raw-pointer identity into non-FFI code); **D** do-nothing. §9.1.4 *Opaque FFI handle types* | 6-0 Q-mechanism A (PLT + AI/ML pivot from B, Min pivot from C); 6-0 Q-null `Option`+guaranteed-NPO; 6-0 Q-construct boundary-sealed; 6-0 Q-cost A cheapest; 6-0 Q-soundness reject C; no Phase D (unanimous); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Opaque-handle mint boundary is E0811's FFI-region set | **Refines the construction clause of the `@ffi.opaque` row above.** The mint boundary the `opaque-ffi-handle` audit body-scan must tag is *exactly* the set of lexical regions where E0811 already permits a `Ptr[T]` — there is **no separate boundary rule**. An **FFI region** is one of three forms: the body of an `@ffi` function, the body of an `@trusted` function, or a `with ffi.scope() as _ { }` block. This **widens** the prior row's "inside `@ffi`/`@trusted`" to also name `ffi.scope` — which the canonical `open()` wrapper (§9.1.4) and E0811's own help text (§07:379) already relied on, so the rule text was stale, not the example. A **mint** is a `Ptr[opaque].deref()` inside a region; a **round-trip** is a handle handed back to a raw pointer there. **Single source of truth**: the region list is stated once with *Pointer Operations* (E0811, §07:211); §9.1.4 and §07:163 point at it rather than restate it, so E0811 and the audit read one predicate and cannot drift. The region is a **purely syntactic**, per-function structural property decided without type resolution (the audit runs pre-typecheck). **Propagation is normative**: a closure literal inside a region inherits it; a **named** nested `fn` item does **not** (needs its own annotation/scope); a pointer that escapes its region dynamically is `PtrEscapesFfiScope` (E0601), not E0811. Canonical `open()` example **unchanged** (correct as written — no `@trusted`). Rejected: **"any `pub fn"** (audit noise, no gate); **"any fn that derefs `Ptr[opaque]"** (circular — redundant with E0811). §9.1.4 *Construction*, §9.1.1 *Pointer Operations* / E0811 rule text (§07:163, :211) | 6-0 Q-region Option A (mint boundary = E0811 region set); 6-0 VAR-1 prose by-reference (single-source, no restatement); 6-0 VAR-2 edit E0811 rule text §163/§211 to name `ffi.scope` (DevOps withdrew `@trusted`-retrofit fallback; PLT: make closure/nested-fn propagation normative); no Phase D (unanimous); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Opaque-handle audit: round-trip and typed completeness | **Replaces the mint-boundary row's "the audit runs pre-typecheck" for site classification.** **One rule for body sites**: in an FFI region, a **mint** is a `.deref()`/`.read()` that loads a value whose type is or contains an `@ffi.opaque` type `H`; a **round-trip** is a `.write(v)` that stores one. **Contains** = `H`, `Option[H]`, or an `@ffi.struct` with a field that contains `H`, at any depth; aliases resolve first; the walk **stops at `Ptr`**; any receiver form counts; **one tag per site per distinct `H`**. A handle passed **by value** to an `@ffi` call is **not** a round-trip: the declaration record covers it once (an opaque param = consume; an opaque return or a `Ptr` out-cell whose pointee contains `H` = produce). The `.write` into the cell is the crossing, not the C call that takes the cell. **Region stays syntactic, classification is typed**: the no-tids lock covers only the region predicate; `blink audit` typechecks first, refuses an ill-typed program (prints errors, exits non-zero), and classifies from checked types. Contract is **exact**; the `ptr_env` heuristic is retired. **E0811 covers bodies**: every expression whose checked type is a `Ptr[T]` must be in a region (implementation is a separate ticket that blocks the audit property test); the `src/cli.bl` "intentionally broader" comment goes. **Handler `fn` clauses inherit the region** like closure literals (walked with the enclosing env); an escaping handler is E0601's concern. The named nested `fn` rule stays, says such items do not parse today, and excludes handler clauses. Today only the plain `H` case can occur: `Ptr[Option[H]]` is E0810 and an opaque `@ffi.struct` field is rejected (§9.1.3). Rejected: **Q1-A** (plain `Ptr[H].write` only — misses field, `Option` and struct-pointee loads); **Q2-O** (stay pre-typecheck, tag unknown receivers `?` — noisy, alias-unsound, makes the property test trivial); **H1-drop**. §9.1.1 *Pointer Operations*, §9.1.4 *Audit category `opaque-ffi-handle`* | Q1 6-0 Q1-B (one load/store rule, V1-V5) + V6 6-0; Q2 6-0 Q2-T (min switched from O) + E1/E2/E3 6-0; Q3 6-0 Q3-H; H1-keep 5-1 (Web dissent: drop the sentence about a feature that does not exist); no Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Operators / methods on `Self` in a trait default body are licensed by the trait's guarantee set | In a trait default method body, `self` has the abstract type `Self`, treated as an **implicit type parameter bounded by the trait's guarantee set**: the methods the trait declares, plus every method of each trait in its supertrait clause, transitively. An operator (desugared to a trait method per *Operator Desugaring* §3.6) or a plain method call on `Self` is well-formed **only when its backing trait is in the guarantee set** — the *Polymorphic Trait Implementations* parametricity rule (§3.6:2330) applied to `Self`, matching the sibling def-site check for declared type parameters. **Reported at the trait definition** (Q2), before any implementor exists, closing the hole where `self + self` on a user type slipped past the typechecker and died at the C compiler. **Bound-required, not permit-then-check-later** (Q1): a body that type-checks under the guarantee set is valid for every implementor, so the check is complete at the definition and never re-run per implementor or per monomorphization. **Sealed-arithmetic consequence is intended, no carve-out** (Q3): the arithmetic traits (`Add`/`Sub`/`Mul`/`Div`/`Rem`/`Neg`) are sealed to built-in numerics, so `trait Doubler: Add` is legal but restricts implementors to the numerics; `self + self` on `Self` is therefore never satisfiable by a user type. **Uniform over every operator family + plain method calls** (Q4); a call to one of the trait's own methods is always licensed (`Eq.ne`→`self.eq()`, `Display.display`→`self.fmt()`). **Two-branch diagnostic `error[UnlicensedSelfOperation]` (E0305)**: an **open** backing trait (`<`→`Ord`, `==`→`Eq`) offers the one-click supertrait fix (`trait Ranked: Ord`); a **sealed** backing trait must **not** suggest `: Add` (an unsatisfiable repair, Q5=B) — it names the seal and offers make-required / concrete-numeric instead. §3 *Operations on `Self` in a Default Body*; catalog E0305 | Q1 6-0 bound-required; Q2 6-0 report at trait definition; Q3 6-0 sealed consequence intended (no carve-out); Q4 6-0 uniform over operators + methods; Q5 6-0 B (do not suggest `: Add`); Phase A.5 dedupe → one option (all six converged), Phase B/D skipped; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| `Void` vs the unit type `()` — `()` is the unit type; `Void` is the FFI-only `Ptr[Void]` marker | Reconciles the §9.1.1-vs-impl contradiction (`Void` restricted to `Ptr` yet ~90 normative `Result[Void, E]` uses). **`()` is THE unit type** — the inhabited, encodable "no meaningful value" type (one value, a representation; usable as return / field / generic argument, `Result[(), E]`, `Map[Str, ()]`). **`Void` is retired to the FFI-only marker for C's incomplete `void` pointee**: well-formed **only** as the argument of `Ptr[_]` (`Ptr[Void]` = `void*`), with no value representation. Every non-`Ptr` use of `Void` (standalone type, parameter, return, field, generic argument) is **`error[VoidOutsidePtr]` (E0828)** whose fix points at `()`. Generic-at-`()` allowed; generic-at-`Void` rejected. `Ptr[Void].deref()` / `.write()` stay **E0825** — a *soundness consequence* of `Void` having no value, not a lint. **Formulation: a formation rule, not a reified kind (C-lite)** — one well-formedness predicate ("`Void` well-formed only as `Ptr`'s argument, else E0828"); the `()`-is-a-value / `Void`-is-a-`void*`-pointee kind distinction survives only as rationale prose (Blink has no user-facing kind system). **Refines the 6-0 *Under-determined types* ruling**: the "ordinary inhabited, encodable" unit is `()`, not `Void` — a naming correction, not a reversal (the I0001 backstop still keys on *kind*, never the `"Void"` tag). Migration: one atomic mechanical codemod of ~92 non-`Ptr` `Result[Void, E]` / `-> Void` sites (spec §§2.20 / 3c / 3.8 / 4.x / 6 done; lib/std + src pending impl), gated by self-host regen + `task ci`. **Supersedes** the `Result[Void, TestError]` spelling in the `?`-in-test-bodies + `prop_check` rows and the `Result[Void, FsError]` spelling in the `fs` row above → now `Result[(), …]`. Rejected: **A / A′** (keep `Void` legal in value position — permanent split corpus / misnames the inhabited unit against ML/Haskell canon), **B** (permanent alias — E0825 silently breaks), **D** (delete `Void` at FFI — `Ptr[()]` lowers to `calloc(1, sizeof(void))`, a C constraint violation). §3.8 *Unit Type*, §9.1.1 *The `Void` type* / E0828 | Q1 5-1 C (devops → A′: keep `Void` as canonical named unit, zero migration); Q2 6-0 C-lite (formation rule, no reified kind); Q3 5-1 Yes reinterpret 6-0 (devops → No, conditional on A′ — self-resolving once C won Q1); no Phase D (all results ≥5-1); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Loop-tail fall-through in value-returning functions | Closes a memory-safety hole: a `fn -> R` whose tail loop could complete normally passed the declared-return check, fell off the end of the emitted C function, and returned stack garbage (`fn h(n) -> Int { while n>0 { if n>0 { return 7 } } }` printed garbage), while `test_loop_return_type.bl` had asserted acceptance since 2026-03-27. **Disposition (Q1): static reject**, never accept-and-fabricate — a fabricated zero-init return collides with the 6-0 *Under-determined types* ruling (E0301) and a fall-through runtime panic re-imports the untracked-divergence the 5-0 `panic → Never` ruling keeps out of the static contract. **Mechanism / scope (Q2): one general body-completeness rule** — a block in value position has a *tail type*; a `fn -> R` type-checks only when `tail <: R`, with `Never <: R` for all `R` and `()` a subtype only of `()`. Deletes the bespoke `infer_loop_return_type` return-scan; covers loop, `if`-without-`else`, and completing-arm tails uniformly (no loop-only carve-out that would leave the other tails as future holes). **Divergence (Q5): loop-only** — a construct is `Never` only when it provably diverges (`panic`/`env.exit`, a tail `return`/`?`/`break`/`continue`, an `if`/`match` whose reachable arms all diverge, or a `loop {}` no `break` targets); `while`/`for` are always `()`, and `while true` is **not** special (no condition-folding — the linter bridges it to `loop`). The break-target search does not descend nested loops or closures. **Diagnostic (Q3): one dedicated code** `error[MissingReturn]` (E0311) — headline names the cause ("this function can finish without returning a value of type `R`"), tail-kind carried as structured diagnostic **data** not a per-tail-kind family, first-named repair (trailing `return`/value, or `Option[R]` + `None`) always compiles. **Codegen rider (Q4): yes** — `__builtin_unreachable()` after an accepted diverging tail in release + a debug-only ICE backstop, never a release runtime panic. §2.11 *Loop typing and divergence*, §3.3 *Divergence and Body Completeness*; catalog E0311 | Q1 6-0 static reject; Q2 6-0 general body-completeness rule; Q3 3-3 → Phase D 4-2 NEW dedicated code (min flipped REUSE→NEW breaking the tie; sys/plt dissent, one-code-not-a-family constraint recorded); Q4 6-0 unreachable + debug ICE; Q5 5-1 loop-only (devops → while-true-as-Never); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| `assert_panics` in a closure written in a test body — the fence is lexical, no new surface | Answers the `@test_only` transitive-helper-extraction request with **no new language surface (Q1, 6-0)**: `@test_only` with call-graph propagation had **zero proposers and six rejections**, and the panel resolved the ticket as a **spec-correction package** instead. **The `assert_panics` fence is lexical** — the compiler checks where the construct is *written*, not where it runs — so a closure written inside `test { }` may contain `assert_panics`, **both** as a direct call argument and `let`-bound, and accepting both is deliberate (Q2). **The closure must not outlive its `test` block** (no module-level state, no field store); passing it directly as an argument does **not** by itself satisfy this, because a parameter's type does not say whether the callee keeps the value — **the compiler does not check this**, stated as a disclosed prohibition rather than an enforced rule. **Undefined behaviour is scoped to the failing half (A3, 5-1)**: F46 established that the two halves longjmp to *different* buffers — a **passing** assertion unwinds to `__blink_panic_jmp`, a `__thread` buffer the construct arms **inline in its own frame** (the runner only *resets* `__blink_panic_armed`), while **E0831/E0832 failures** route through `__blink_assert_fail_intro` to `__blink_test_jmp`, a bare `static` set only in the runner loop. So only the *failing* assertion in an escaped closure is undefined; Systems' wider wording (A2) would have called the defined half undefined. **The §2.20:1417 rationale is corrected (Q3, 4-1-1)**: its claim that a recognized block keeps the panic continuation observable only by the test runner is **false as shipped** (F19 — a closure containing `assert_panics` stored in module-level `let mut` escapes and is callable from ordinary code). Replaced with the creation-site property, which is the only one enforced: a catch frame can only be **created** lexically inside `test { }` (E0833); nothing confines the resulting function value afterwards. **E0833's help names no closure shape (Q4, 6-0)** and its prescribed repair becomes callee extraction, named first — the shipped help and explainer both prescribe "move it into a `test { ... }` block", the repair this ticket exists because users cannot apply (F31/F36), and both `blink explain E0833` examples fail `blink check` (F32). **No LSP quick fix** (`lsp_handle_code_action` emits one TextEdit over the diagnostic's own range; every repair here moves code between scopes). **`@expect_panic` on a plain `fn` is rejected at the annotation via the existing E1110 (Q5, 6-0)**, generalized off its type-alias wording, and `@expect_panic` is **spec'd, not deleted**. **Unknown-annotation diagnostic is out of scope (Q6, 4-2)** — it gates nothing now that no annotation proposal remains, and `@test_only` is **not** reserved by name (6-0: reserving the name just declined would signal intent to add it). **Sequencing (Q7, 6-0)**: the reopen gate merges Minimalism M6 and PLT item 9 only; Web's §2.20 defect ledger stays separate (corrections do not carry the burden additions carry). Also carried 6-0: no Blink example enters `sections/*.md` for this decision unless it passes `blink check` or is marked an intentional-error example; §2.20 Pattern 1's unannotated `for_each` closure parameter is repaired now rather than waiting behind the gate. **Recorded, not amended**: §4.6.3's third ground ("exposes no user-nameable symbol") **does not hold as shipped** — true of the construct, false of a closure containing one — deferred to the escape-analysis follow-up; this panel did **not** amend §4.6.3 and did **not** adopt an escape analysis. **Limit on the record**: the `assert_panics` emit site is absent from this tree (the codegen rewrite removed it), so F46 was read from the runtime headers and the normative decision record and **could not be run**; A3's scoping is to be re-checked once the emitters land. §2.20 *`assert_panics`* (closure-in-test-body paragraphs, corrected *Why a block, not a closure*, repaired Pattern 1) | Q1 6-0 A (no new surface); Q2 base text 5-1 (min dissent: preferred the shorter disclosed-prohibition rung), amendment A1 6-0 (strike the non-expressible escape route, F41), consequence clause 3-3 → Phase D 5-1 A3 after F46 (plt and min flipped A2→A3 on independent verification; **sys dissent**: A3 promotes an implementation choice into a spec guarantee the runtime then owes, and keys on a run-time outcome the reader cannot act on at the store site); Q3 4-1-1 C (**sys dissent**: wanted a normative requirement on the lowering, routed to a ticket by unanimous Concern fields; **web** relocates the thread-local clause to §4.6.3, 2-4); Q4 3-2-1 no majority → Phase D rung 6-0 A, text `Q4-A-devops+A4` 6-0; Q5 6-0 A; Q6 4-2 C (**devops dissent** on warning severity, 5-1); Q7 6-0 option 3; sub-items Q2-1/Q3-2/Q3-3/Q4-1/Q4-2/Q5-1/Q5-2/Q6-2/Q7-1a/Q7-1b/Q7-1c all 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Four call-shape rules the compiler enforced and the spec never stated (`BareGenericFnAsValue`, `InvalidKeywordArg`, `FfiScopeNotWithResource`, `RawBypassesParam`) | **Three of the four rules were wrong as written and were changed, not documented.** **E0517 `BareGenericFnAsValue` is deleted (Q1, 6-0)**: a generic function's name is a value wherever a function value is expected, and its binders are solved by unifying the declaration's type against the expected type; the residual — nothing fixes the binders — falls to `CannotInferType` (E0301) with the **annotation** form as its first help, never a bracket form while `CallSiteTypeArgs` stands. The rule was a syntactic test on the AST node (`node_kind == Ident`): it refused `apply(identity, 3)` and accepted `let f = identity; apply(f, 3)` one line apart, and its shipped repair `{ x => identity(x) }` does not parse under §2.8. **Both §2.13 rules are now enforced by `blink check` (Q2-enf, 5-1)** — a label on a positional parameter is rejected, and a keyword parameter supplied without its label is rejected — where the compiler previously enforced **neither**, in both directions, while §2.13 stated both normatively. A label resolves in the callee's **keyword-parameter namespace**; variant-payload and struct-literal labels name **fields** and are outside the rule. **The call-site label mistakes divide into five codes (Q2-count, 6-0)**: `MissingKeywordArg` (E0510, the value is absent), `UnlabeledKeywordArg` (E0529, the value is present and unlabelled — the only one whose fix is machine-applicable), `InvalidKeywordArg` (E0511), `PositionalAfterKeyword` (E0527), `DuplicateKeywordArg` (E0528). **E0819 `FfiScopeNotWithResource` is restated over the `FfiScope` *type*, not the `ffi.scope()` call (Q3, 6-0)** — an `FfiScope` may occur only as the resource of a `with ... as` block and nowhere else (not `let`-bound, passed, returned, stored in a field, or written as a type argument), because the hazard is a libc `malloc`/`free` arena belonging to the value, and a call-site rule is walked past by any binding. It is an **error** where the `Closeable`-without-scope rule is a warning because a misused `FfiScope` releases **nothing**. **`Raw[T]` becomes a real marker type (Q4, 6-0; 5 of 6 blocking-conditional)** — it was documented as "a compiler-known marker type" and implemented as the identity function, so `let t = Raw(table)` laundered past the warning entirely. It now has **no `Display`**, the marker survives a binding, nothing strips it implicitly, and `RawBypassesParam` fires on the `Template[C]` coercion and only there. The blocking condition — met — is `RawOutsideTemplate` (E0530), because a misplaced `Raw()` would otherwise fall to `MissingDisplayImpl`, whose three repairs are all impossible on a marker type. **Audit-gated diagnostics are stated once, in §9.1 (Q4-supp-allow, 6-0 — was 3-3 before the third option was offered)**: `@trusted(audit: K)` is the sole suppression channel for `UnauditedFfi` and `RawBypassesParam`; `@allow(Name)` and `[lints]` do not reach them and naming one there is **refused, not ignored**. Membership criterion: silently suppressing it would destroy a record the language promises exists. Unsilenceable means **no channel that records nothing**, not no channel at all — and this **removes a capability that works today**, which release notes must say in those words. `@trusted` without a non-empty `audit:` is `TrustedRequiresAudit` (E0836). **`blink audit --raw-queries` and `--no-unaudited-raw` are struck from the spec** — neither flag exists; the tool is filed separately and the sentence returns when it ships. §2.13 (*Call-Site Diagnostics*), §3.4 (*A generic function named as a value*), §3b.5 (`Raw[T]`), §9.1 (*Audit-Gated Diagnostics*), §9.1.1 (*The `FfiScope` type*), ERROR_CATALOG.md | Q1 6-0 A; Q2-count 6-0 for the four-code split over the three-code one — the contested fourth is `UnlabeledKeywordArg`, which no three-code scheme catches; with the pre-existing `MissingKeywordArg` the family is five (all six votes conditional on Q2-enf); Q2-enf **5-1** (**plt dissent**: the keyword check has one call site, the bare-Ident free-function path, so enforcement would hold for `f(x: 1)` and not `b.f(x: 1)` — non-compositionality, the same defect that killed E0517; recorded at plt's request in §2.13's *Extent of enforcement*, with the method-path fix a blocking prerequisite on the implementation ticket); Q3 6-0 B; Q4 6-0 A (5 of 6 blocking-conditional on a dedicated misplaced-`Raw` diagnostic in the same change set — devops alone unconditional); Q4-supp-allow 6-0 (3-3 in round 2, flipped by devops's `[lints]` finding); Q4-supp-rest 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Diagnostic codes for a wrong type-argument count at a call, and for brackets that are not a call (`f[Int]`, `xs[1]`) | **E0303 `TypeArgArity` widens to every written type-argument list (Q1, 6-0)**, callees included: a wrong count at a call is the same failure as in a type position, so it gets the same code, not E0301 (nothing is unbound). A callee and a struct-literal head are **term positions**, where an absent list is left to inference; this also resolves the §3.4 contradiction between the struct-literal head listed as a type position and `W { n: 1 }` inferring. A rejected list supplies nothing, so no E0301 or E0306 fires for that call's binders. E0303 is decided when the head resolves: at name resolution for a path, once the receiver's type is known for a method. The first help is "delete the list" when inference then fixes every binder. **Brackets that are not a type application get two new codes, chosen by the bracket contents (Q2, 6-0)**: a value inside is `NoIndexOperator` (E0313), with or without a call after it; all types is checked for arity first (E0303), then `TypeArgsWithoutCall` (E0314) if no `(` or `{` follows. The contents are known at name resolution in every case, so `xs[i]` and `self.items[i]` get the same code at the same stage. E0314's first help removes the brackets only when an expected type fixes the same arguments that were written; `fns[0](x)` gets a note and no machine fix. **E0307 `CallSiteTypeArgs` is retired and never reused (Q3, 6-0)**, recorded in a new Retired Codes table. It shipped with the opposite meaning (brackets at a call are refused). The spec's own index examples (`xs[0].`, `mc.elapsed()[1]`, `tags["rare"] = 1`) are fixed in the same change. §2.6, §3.4 (*Explicit Type Application*, *Kind-Correctness of Type Expressions*, *Postfix Brackets That Are Not a Type Application*), ERROR_CATALOG.md | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0 for CONTENTS (round 1 split 3 CONTENTS / 2 UNION / 1 HEAD; min withdrew a parse-error option E1100); Q2 numbering, E0314 help (with devops's guard), and the no-machine-fix index help each 6-0; Q3 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| FFI scope tags (`Ptr[T]^σ` / `Buf[T]^σ` on parameters) — inferred, no syntax | No surface σ; `^σ` is spec meta-notation and `^` stays XOR. The compiler infers a scope tag per pointer-bearing value (binder node, not name). `E0601 (tag-mismatch)` = a tagged value stored into a cell whose scope it does not enclose, by a closed list of four store forms: write, escaping capture, effect argument, call beside a pointer-holding argument. `@trusted` params share the caller's tag (`ptr-store-param` audit row). `@ffi.struct` gains `Ptr[T]` fields (E0811-gated, transitive) and loses `Buf` fields; `scope.alloc_n` returns `Ptr[T]`. C-side retention stays in the audit trust base. Amends ffi-struct-construction and buf-u8-runtime-representation. See [rationale](decisions/ffi-scope-tag-inference.md) | 6-0 (all 10 questions) |
| Channel `recv()` on a closed, empty channel | **`recv() -> Option[T]` is the one blocking receive (Q1)**; it returns `None` only when the channel is closed **and** drained. Replaces the silent zero (a sent `0` and "closed" were the same answer) and the rewrite codegen's panic (uncatchable under §4.6.3, and with several consumers the one that aborts is chosen by timing). **`Channel[T]` joins the sealed `IntoIterator` table (Q2)**: `for v in ch` is exactly `for v in iter.from_fn(fn() { ch.recv() })`. **Delivery guarantee (Q3)**: a drain receives every sent value, in order; a sent value never ends the stream — closed is a state of the channel, never a value of `T` (`0`, `""`, `false`, `None` on `Channel[Option[T]]` all arrive as `Some`). The runtime ABI stays out of the spec. **`send` on a closed channel panics (Q4)**; one owner closes, after every producer joins. **A second `close()` is a no-op (Q5)**. **Constructor spelling is not part of this ruling (Q6)**: `channel.new[T](buffer: N)` (OPEN_QUESTIONS §2.1) stands; aligning the code's `Channel(n)` is separate work that lands before the §4.13 examples publish. **Bare `sem.recv()` stays legal, no lint (Q7)**; code that must not go on past a closed channel writes `.recv().unwrap()`. Rejected: the zero value; an `is_closed()` pre-check (races with other consumers); `try_recv` as a name for a blocking receive (means non-blocking in Rust, crossbeam, tokio, Kotlin); a panicking `recv() -> T` with a second Option-returning method. §4.13 *Channel operations*, §3c.1 *`for` Loop Desugaring and `IntoIterator`* | 6-0 Q1–Q7 (Web moved B→A and Min moved C→A in Phase B); no Phase D (unanimous); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Scoped effect handler (`with db.connect(path)? { }`) | **A `BlockHandler` whose `Context` is `Handler[E]` is a scoped effect handler (Q1)**: `with` evaluates the item, calls `enter()`, installs the returned handler for `E`, runs the body, uninstalls it, then calls `exit(ok)` under the outer handlers. General, keyed on the type, never on a name; the old `db`+`connect` codegen special case stays gone. **`db.connect(path)` returns `Result[Connection, DBError]` (Q2)**, used as `with db.connect(p)? { }`. **Own row in the §4.7 disambiguation table (Q3)**; without `as` the check order is `Handler[E]`, then `Context == Handler[E]`, then `Context == ()`. **`as` installs and binds (Q4)**; it never changes what the block does. **Comma-separated items nest from left to right (Q5)**: item *i* is evaluated after items 0..*i*-1 are entered; teardown is LIFO. **One flow-based lint, ScopedValueWithoutWith, for every `Closeable` and `BlockHandler` value that does not go into a `with` (Q6)**; its numeric code waits for the W0600 catalog clash. **E0601/E0602 cover the `Handler[E]` that `as` binds (Q7)**. **An abstract `Context` in generic code installs nothing and is E0839 (Q8)**. Rejected: a `ScopedHandler[E]` prelude type (affine "consumed" check does not survive aliasing); a cleanup clause on the `handler E { }` literal (one-shot lifetime on a reusable value); a stdlib `db.open` + `conn.handler()` pair. Deferred: fallible `enter`, `exit(self, ctx, ok)` / lazy open. §4.6.3 *Scoped effect handlers*, §4.7 *Disambiguation rules*, §5.5 | 6-0 Q1, Q2, Q4–Q8; 5-1 Q3 (Min dissent: widen row 2); AI/ML moved Y→X, Sys and Min moved to `Result` in Phase B; no Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Builtin type name shadowing (`type Handle`, `type Handler`) | **Reserve only `Self`, the scalar types literal syntax produces (`Int`, `I8`..`I64`, `U8`..`U64`, `Float`, `F32`, `F64`, `Str`, `Char`, `Bool`) and `Void` (Q1)**: declaring one is error `E0524 ReservedTypeName`; the reserved set grows only when literal syntax grows. **Every other compiler-known name shadows (Q1)**: the user `type`, alias, `trait` or `effect` wins in its module, with `W1010` once at the declaration; `import blink.core.{X as Y}` (or `blink.ffi`) reaches the builtin; diagnostics that involve a shadowing name add a note with both homes, and two same-name types print as `Name (module)`. **`Fn`/`Tuple` are not names (Q2)**: `type Fn` is legal; the compiler must stop tagging annotations with user-writable spellings. **The compiler-known set is closed and frozen (Q3)**: one §10.6 table; new builtins go in named std modules. **Accepted (Q4)**: a type name resolves once to one declaration identity (unresolved = `E0507`; only a `[T]` binder makes a type variable); desugarings and compiler-inserted types use the builtin by identity; `Handler[E]` needs no import; `Duration`/`Instant` are plain `std.time` names; no codegen stage selects behavior by a name string. §3.4 *Type Name Resolution*, §10.6 *Compiler-Known Type Names*, *Shadowing Rules* | 6-0 Q1 R2 (S-lit; R1 was 4-2); 6-0 Q2–Q4; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Evaluation order of arguments, operands, literals and interpolation | **Written order, left to right, for every expression (Q1)**: each operand sub-expression runs exactly once, one at a time, and finishes (side effects and panics included) before the next starts; a variable read is an evaluation at its written position, so `bump() + n * 100` reads `n` after `bump()`; if an operand exits through `?` or a panic, the later ones do not run. Calls: callee, receiver, then arguments as written; **keyword arguments run in written order, not declaration order**, and bind afterwards. An implementation may reorder only when no program can observe it (output, any binding's value including a captured `let mut`, which panic occurs, termination); an empty effect row is not enough. **Closed list of forms that select which parts run (Q2)**: `&&`, `||`, `??`, `if`, `match` (arms and guards), loops (`while`, `loop`, `for`, re-run under the rule each iteration); a closure literal is a value and its body runs at each call. **Compound assignment (Q3)**: `place op= rhs` evaluates the place's sub-expressions once, then reads the current value, then `rhs`, then `op`, then stores; `xs[next()] += 1` calls `next()` once, `n += bump()` reads `n` before `bump()`. Amends the 5-0 purely-syntactic rewrite for non-variable places. **Formatter (Q4)**: strikes §2.13's "formatter enforces declaration order at call sites"; the formatter never reorders arguments, struct-literal fields or list elements; no lint now. **Assignment places (Q5)**: evaluating a place yields its index and key values; the root binding is read, the element found and bounds-checked at the store, after `rhs`. Rejected: order unspecified (the ticket's option (b)): undiagnosable, since effect rows do not record captured-cell writes or overflow panics, and it contradicts E0301. §2.22 *Evaluation Order*, §2.13, §2.16, §2.19 *Assignment Operators*, §2.4 | 6-0 Q1–Q5; nobody proposed (b); Min moved B→A and Sys withdrew C on Q2, DevOps withdrew its S1-b diagnostic and `KeywordArgOrder` lint, Sys withdrew its atoms-only formatter reorder, AI/ML withdrew its compound-assignment wording in Phase B; no Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| `Bool` distinct from `Int` | Delete all three Int/Bool carve-outs in the typechecker (assignability in `types_compatible`, the `==`/`!=` comparison arm, and Int accepted by `is_bool_compat`). **Assignability (Q1)**: `Bool` is not assignable to or from `Int` at let, assign, argument, return, field or element, in both directions. **Conditions (Q2)**: `if`, `while`, match guards, and the operands of `&&`/`\|\|`/`!` take exactly `Bool`, literals included (`while 1 { }` is an error). **Comparison (Q3)**: `==`/`!=` between `Bool` and `Int` is an error, enforced in the same change. This departs from enums, which keep tag comparison: an enum has a numeric identity (`to_int`), a `Bool` has none (`Eq.eq(self, other: Self)`; §3 "a `Bool` never reads as 0 or 1"). **No conversion methods (Q4, Q5)**: no `Bool.to_int()`, no `from_int`/`to_bool`, no cast; `if b { 1 } else { 0 }` and `n != 0` are the spellings, and a `.to_int()` call on a `Bool` gets a help line with the first. **Patterns (Q6)**: an Int pattern on a `Bool` scrutinee and a `true`/`false` pattern on an `Int` scrutinee are errors, enforced now (not staged as the enum rule was); an exhaustive `Bool` match needs no `_`, and codegen must not add a default arm to hide a bad value. **FFI (Q7)**: every `Bool` read from C is `true` or `false`; in an FFI position `Bool` is C `bool` (`_Bool`); a C `int` flag is declared `I32` and converted with `!= 0`. **Soundness dependency (Q8)**: the invariant "every `Bool` is `true` or `false`" also needs struct-literal field values to be type-checked; the rule change may land first, but the ticket closes only after that check lands. **Migration (Q9)**: hard flip, no warning phase: rewrite the sites under the pinned gen0 first (change `-> Int` predicates to `-> Bool`, not wrap them), then delete the carve-outs in one PR. **Diagnostics (Q10)**: E0300, caret under the operand, fixed `note: Blink has no truthiness`, machine-applicable `help:` per shape. Measured: at least 524 sites (src 423, lib/std 27, lib/pkg 1, tests 73), most from about 25 `-> Int` predicates. §3.4 *`Bool` Is Distinct from `Int`*, §2.19 *No Truthiness*, §9.1.3 *`Bool` at the FFI boundary*, §3.6 | 6-0 Q1–Q7a, Q8–Q10; 5-1 Q7b (Min dissent: same answer, but the layout change should not share a diff with the type flip; the five in the majority all require a separate layout PR, so no Phase D); Sys and PLT withdrew `Bool.to_int()`, and Sys, PLT and Min withdrew keep-`==`-for-enum-parity, in Phase B; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| `Display.fmt` effect row and impl effect-row rule | **`fmt` has no effect row; the empty row is the contract (Q1)**. There is no `StringBuilderPure` effect: an effect row grants capabilities and cannot restrict them. Amends Display locked point 4. **Every impl of a trait method, required or open default, must declare a row that is a subset of the trait method's row (Q4)**; widening is `error[TraitContractEffectMismatch]` (E0904). Before this, the rule covered only open-default overrides, and `impl Display for Pt { fn fmt(self, sb: StringBuilder) ! IO {...} }` passed the checker. Delete the orphan `pub trait StringBuilderPure` in lib/std (Q2). Strike the unchecked claims "cannot read external state", "allocate observably", "referentially transparent", "cache-friendly" (Q3). Module-level `let mut` in `fmt` gets no spec rule; a writes-only tooling lint over Display.fmt/Eq.eq/Hash.hash/Ord.cmp is a feature ticket (Q5; **superseded by the *Module-level state effect* ruling below**: such an impl is now E0904). The `sb_write_char` FFI shim takes `Char` (Q6) | 6-0 (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4, Q6); Q5 3-3 R1 → 6-0 R2 for no spec rule plus a tooling ticket |

Element writes are method calls under rule 4 (Q4). | Scalar → `Str`: `display()` only, no `Int.to_str` | **Display only (Q1)**: `display()` and interpolation are the only ways to turn `Int`, `Float` or `Bool` into a `Str`; the typecheck rows for `Int.to_str`/`to_string` and `Float.to_string` go. On the scalar types a `to_` method is sugar over `From`/`TryFrom` (`.to_X()` / `.to_X_checked()`), so `Char.to_str` stays (`From[Char] for Str`) and `Int.to_str` does not exist. `to_str`/`to_string`/`toString` on `Int`, `Float` or `Bool` is E0505 with a machine-applicable fix to `.display()`; the §2 examples use `.display()`. **No warning period (Q2)**: add the fix, apply it repo-wide, then remove the rows (add → migrate → remove). | `unwrap` / `unwrap_err` panic bound and message | **Debug bound on the arm that panics (Q1)**: `Result.unwrap` needs `E: Debug`, `Result.unwrap_err` needs `T: Debug`, `Option.unwrap` has none; checked at each call, E0306, never inferred from a generic body; the message renders with `debug()`; no `<TypeName>` placeholder. **General rule (Q2)**: any `Result`/`Option` method that panics on an arm needs `Debug` on that arm, binding on later methods; `expect` is not added here (follow-up ticket). **Normative text (Q3)**: `unwrap called on Err: ` + `debug(e)`, `unwrap_err called on Ok: ` + `debug(v)`, `unwrap called on None`; the `panic: ` lead-in and ` at <file>:<line>` suffix are not normative. **N1**: stdlib error types derive `Debug` (keep `Display`) before the bound is enforced. **N2**: E0514 help names `@derive(Debug)` + `.unwrap()`. **N3**: test-body `?` Display→Debug is a follow-up. **N4**: `unwrap_or`, `unwrap_or_else`, `?`, `??`, `match` carry no bound; E0306 help order `@derive(Debug)`/`: Debug`, then `match` + `panic`, then `unwrap_or` (no panic); `@derive` fix-it only for a user type. **N5**: supersedes unwrap-panic-message-format.md. **N6**: spec examples audited. §7.5, §2.20, §3.6.1 | 6-0 Q1–Q3; N1–N6 confirmed 6-0; no Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Unhandled effect operation | **Compile error at the roots, panic at run time (Q1)**: a user-declared effect that reaches `main` with no `with` that discharges it is a compile error; an operation that finds no handler at run time (a partial handler, or `default.op()` with no outer handler) panics. No operation answers a default value. **One code (Q2)**: `UnhandledEffect` (E0539) for `main` and test blocks; the header names the root; `UnhandledEffectInTest` folds into it. **§4.6 (Q3)**: keep "implicitly holds all effects"; add that built-in effects have root handlers and user-declared effects do not. **Behaviour only (Q4)**: the operation panics and never returns, `-> Never` included; the C lowering (one stub per operation with its exact signature, never one stub cast to every signature) is in the decision record. **Spawn (Q5)**: a task uses the handlers in force at its `async.spawn` site; spawn is not a root. **Panic message (Q6)**: contains the operation as called, `<handle>.<op>` (e.g. `cache.put`); other wording and the location are not specified. §4.6, §4.7.1, §4.12, §4.13, §2.20; ERROR_CATALOG.md E0539 | Q1, Q2, Q4, Q5 6-0; Q3 5-1 (plt: reword §4.6); **Q6 4-2** after 3-3 (plt: declared names; sys: fixed prefix + declared name) at the Phase D cap; AI-First 5/5 pass |

Only a callee head, never a type or a value; a type that lacks the impl is E0306 at the head; resolution fixed at type check. **No `Self` inference (Q2)**: `let w: Widget = Alpha.make()` stays an error whose help gives the impl-qualified form; inference may land later as a conservative extension. **Receiver methods legal, not promoted (Q3)**: `(Display for User).display(u)` = `Display.display(u)`; help and docs print `Trait.method(x)`; `blink fmt` rewrites neither form. **E0522 help (Q4)**: receiver calls keep `Trait.method(x)`; an associated fn gets one pasteable impl-qualified line per owner, type params under their own name, generic traits as full instances; rename moves to the explain text. **Swapped head (Q5)**: E0740 `QualifiedHeadNotTrait`; when the left is a type and the right a trait, the help gives the swapped head, never a swap otherwise; content fixed, not wording. §3c.4 *Impl-Qualified Calls*, catalog E0740 | 6-0 Q1; 6-0 Q2; 6-0 Q3; 6-0 Q4; 5-1 Q5 (min: the swap help is implementation quality, not spec) |
| Effect leaf operations | **Leaf effects declare operations (Q1)**: `effect Store { fn get(key: Str) -> Int }` is legal; the flat examples in §4.6, §4.7.1 and §7 stay. **Never both (Q2)**: every effect body, built-in or user-declared, holds operations or sub-effects, never both; a mixed body is `MixedEffectBody` (E0541, Q4) with a fix that moves the operations into a new sub-effect with a placeholder name. §4.4.3 `Env` moves `exit` into a child `effect Exit` — an explicit amendment of the *env.exit() placement* and *Env sub-effect granularity* rows above; every program that compiles today keeps its meaning. **Depth (Q3)**: v1 trees have the top-level effect and one level of sub-effects; a grandchild is `EffectNestingTooDeep` (E0542); the ops rule is worded for any depth and the compiler walks the tree with one recursive collector. **Unique op names (Q5)**: one tree, one namespace; a duplicate is `DuplicateEffectOp` (E0543), naming both declarations. **`effect X {}` = `effect X` (Q6)**; `blink fmt` rewrites to the bare form; braces that hold a comment stay. **Grammar (Q7)**: `effect_decl`/`effect_body`/`child_effect` productions in §4.12. **E0538 note (Q8)**: `! Store.get` lists the leaf's operations and suggests `! Store` (guidance, not normative). **Leaf-to-tree split (Q9)**: rows, handlers and calls stay valid. §4.3, §4.4.3, §4.12; catalog E0541–E0543 | 6-0 Q1, Q3–Q9; Q2 6-0 in Phase D (was 4-2 in Phase C: web and min for Q2-c, a built-in exception for Env); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Code for `ScopedValueWithoutWith` (the W0600 clash) | **`UnusedVariable` keeps W0600; `ScopedValueWithoutWith` is W0610 (Q1)**, with Category Ranges rows `W060x \| Linting` and `W061x \| Resource scope lints` (Q1b). **`CloseableWithoutScope` goes in the Retired table with Code "—" (Q2)**: the catalog published the name, but no compiler emitted it; "—" means that. **`blink explain W0600` gets one line saying the code never meant the scoped-value lint (V1)**. **The W0610 constant and explain entry land with the emit site, not before (V2)**; the catalog row and §5.5 reserve the code now. **A `task ci` check (V3)** compares each emitted constant's (name, code) pair with its catalog row, keeps names and codes unique, keeps a code out of both the live and Retired tables, and matches structured forms in sections/ and llms-full.md (`warning[Name]`/`error[Name]` headers, a code beside a backticked name, `[lints]` keys, `@allow` arguments) with no allowlist. **llms-full shows `@allow(UnusedVariable)` (V5)**. Deferred: the "§6" spec-ref on W0600–W0602 (V4). Rejected: W0605; a Conventions rule that only an emitted name is published (it would leave `ScopedValueWithoutWith` unreserved). | Selective import and module-level declaration of one name | **Compile error (Q1)**: a module-level declaration (`type`, alias, `trait`, `effect`, `fn`, module `let`, both namespaces) of a name that a selective import binds is an error; the check uses the name after `as` (`{Point as P}` + `type P` is an error, `{Point as P}` + `type Point` is fine). **Widened E1012 `DuplicateSymbol` (Q2)**: covers `import` and `pub import`; "public" and "re-export" leave its title. **Now (Q3)**: no warning phase; src/ and lib/std/ fixed in the same change. **Fix in the spec (Q4)**: dropping the name from a plain `import` is machine-applicable, a suggestion for `pub import`; an emptied list becomes `import alpha` unless no resolved name uses the qualifier; E1012 replaces W0602 on that entry. **§10.6 (Q5)**: shadowing happens only between nested scopes, two module-scope bindings are a duplicate; `import blink.core.{Handler}` + `type Handler` is E1012, not W1010; the §10.1 qualifier rule (`let auth = 5`) is a different layer. | Module qualifier and module-level declaration | **One table (Q)**: every import binds its qualifier (leaf name, or the name after `as`) in both namespaces; a module-level declaration of that name (`type`, alias, `trait`, `effect`, `fn`, `const`, `let`, `let mut`) is E1012 `DuplicateSymbol`, for `import` and `pub import`; "binds no bare name" is deleted. **Span (V1)**: primary on the declaration, the import entry as a label, one E1012 per colliding entry. **Selective imports (V2)**: `import gamma.{Point}` binds `gamma` too. **Cascade (V3)**: fix is a suggestion; E1012 replaces W0602; use-site errors caused only by the collision are not reported; recovery binding not specified. **§10.6 (V4)**: "an import entry and a declaration". **No case rule for `as` (V5)**. **Locals (L)**: a local binding hides a qualifier for its scope, legal, no binder warning; `x.y` resolves against the local only, no fallback (L1); binder list in §2.2 *Local bindings*. **Notes (L2–L4)**: §10.6 shadowing-note item: a use-site failure under a hide names the hidden module when it has a `pub` item of that name; W0602 gives the first hiding binder when every `q.y` resolves to a local; no W0602 when the hide note fired. **No binder W-code (L5)**. §2.2, §10.1, §10.5, §10.6, catalog E1012 | 6-0 Q, V1–V5, L, L1, L3, L5; L2 5-1 (Min: chore, not spec); **L4 6-0 in Phase D** after 3-3 (sys, plt, min: plain W0602); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Selective import qualifier collision | **Eager (Q-T)**: a selective import binds its qualifier, so `import a.x.{P}` + `import b.x.{Q}` is E1005 on `x` at the import, used or not; selective + whole-module with one leaf is the same E1005 **(Q-S2)**. **Qualifier alias (Q-G)**: `import b.x as bx.{Q}` binds `Q` and `bx`; `as` renames the thing on its left. **Same module again (Q-S1)**: no E1005 and no W0602 for the qualifier; W0602 only for a duplicate listed item (or a whole-module import whose qualifier already names its module). **Wrong order (Q-P)**: `import b.x.{Q} as bx` is E1128 `QualifierAliasAfterList`, machine-applicable fix to G-PRE that keeps the list, no second diagnostic. **Text (Q-S3, Q-A)**: §10.5 *Imported Names* lists the selective qualifier; *Import Aliases* shows every form with what it binds. §10.1, §10.5, catalog E1005, E1128 | 6-0 Q-T, Q-S1, Q-S2, Q-S3, Q-A; Q-G 5-1 (Min: no new grammar, defer the form); Q-P 5-0 (Min N/A, yes if a form wins); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Module-scope name collision and code drift | **One rule**: in module scope each bare name has one declaration per namespace (value: `let`, `let mut`, `const`, `fn`; type: `type`, alias, `trait`, `effect`); two `fn`s of one name are an error whatever the signatures, with no winner. **Codes (Q1)**: E1016 `DuplicateModuleBinding` for two declarations, E1012 for import + declaration, E1005 for import + import; §10.6 states the rule once and names all three. **Drift fixed**: §2.12.1 E1009 → E1016; `PubLetMutForbidden` E1006 → **E1013 (Q2)**, constant reserved in src/diagnostics.bl, check not yet enforced **(Q2b)**; stale §10.8 *Compilation Model Errors* table deleted, private item access is E1003 **(Q3)**; *Import Errors* table gains E1006, E1007, E1013, E1015, E1016, E1003 row renamed *Private item access* **(Q4)**. **Gate (Q5)**: spec-vs-src code check is a separate chore. **Diagnostic (Q6)**: span on the later declaration, N-1 diagnostics, reported at name resolution, bind to first only to recover, suggestion-only fix, no C-symbol reason. §2.12.1, §10.2, §10.5, §10.6, §10.8 | 6-0 rule, Q1, Q2b, Q4, Q6; Q2 5-1 (Systems: E1017); Q3 5-1 (Web: fix table in place); Q5 4-2 (DevOps, AI/ML: land gate now), soft consensus, user approved; AI-First 5/5 pass |

Applies to `Errno` (today `Errno(5) == 5` compares the payload and a user `Code(5) == 5` emits invalid C). **Layout is not spec**: the spec states no layout for any enum or newtype (user ruling: implementation details do not go in the spec); codegen lowers by shape and the declaration-keyed `Errno` table goes. §07 layout wording (`Errno` "no boxing, no tag", `MsgFlags` "one `int64_t`") removed. `Errno.code()` stays promised by §07, tracked separately. §3.4 *Enums Are Nominally Distinct from `Int`* | 6-0 Q1; 5-1 Q1b `.code()` follow-up (Web dissent); Q2-Q4 G (size guarantee) 5-1 → overruled by user (out of spec); Q5 4-2 R1 → 3-2-1 R2 (B2/A/B1) → user chose B2 (out of spec); AI-First 4/5 pass |
| Runtime libraries `c` and `m` in `@ffi` | **Implicit (Q1)**: `@ffi("c", ...)` and `@ffi("m", ...)` need no `[native-dependencies]` entry; `c` and `m` are the §9.1.2 runtime row (libc, libm), and that row is the whole implicit set (no pthreads, no sqlite3). **Manifest key (Q2)**: a key `c`, `m`, `libc` or `libm` is E0841 `CompilerManagedNativeDep`, span on the `blink.toml` key, machine-applicable fix deletes the line. **One spelling (Q3)**: `@ffi("libc"/"libm", ...)` is E0820 with a machine-applicable fix to `"c"`/`"m"` that changes only the library argument; §9.1.1 `getenv` example fixed. **No manifest (Q4)**: a program with no `blink.toml` is checked as if `[native-dependencies]` were empty; the E0820 help then shows a complete file to create. **One-argument form (Q5)**: `@ffi("sym")` binds a symbol from `c` and `m`, needs no entry, adds no link flag; `header:` keeps its meaning; prefer the two-argument form outside `lib/std`. Also fixes the dangling §10.5.4 reference on gate #275 and the §9.1 example that bound a runtime-internal symbol under `"c"`. §9.1, §9.1.1, §9.1.2, catalog E0841 | 6-0 Q1–Q4; Q5 5-1 (Min: 5B, leave the one-argument form to its own gap); Q5 wording 5A-i 5-1 (Min: 5A-ii); AI-First 4/5 pass (Generability: two forms for runtime symbols) |
| A bracket item whose name is both a type and a value | A name in a bracket item resolves **in the type namespace only**, the same as in a type annotation (§3.4 *Postfix Brackets That Are Not a Type Application*). A value of the same name, at module scope or as a local `let`, does not change the result: with `type MAX` and `const MAX` both in scope, `make[MAX]()` compiles and `xs[MAX]` is **E0303**. Names inside a compound item (`xs[List[MAX]]`) are in type position too. **No spec diagnostic or lint** for a name in both namespaces (tooling may add one). **Help order is normative**: when the head is a value and the item is a bare name that also has a value binding, the first `help:` is the element read (`.get()` or a tuple field, by the E0313 receiver rule) and "delete the list" is second; a `note:` names both declarations. **An unresolved name** in an item gets only the name-resolution error; E0313, E0303 and E0314 are suppressed for that suffix, nested names included. Rejected: value-first, innermost-scope-first, and the next-token-plus-generic-head tie-break (N-g), which needs the receiver type for a method head such as `scope.alloc[MAX]()`. | Q1 type namespace only: 5-1 in Phase D after 3-3 in Phase C (DevOps dissent: N-g gives the right code, not only the right first help); Q2 no lint 6-0; Q3 note + `.get` first 6-0; Q4 unresolved name suppresses bracket codes 6-0 |
| Signed `MIN % -1` and the remainder definition | **`MIN % -1` is 0, no panic (Q1)**: the overflow rule panics only when a result exceeds the type's range, and 0 is in range; `MIN / -1` still panics. **`%` is defined in the same edit (Q2)**: for a non-zero `b`, `a % b` is `a - b * q`, `q` the exact quotient truncated toward zero, over the mathematical integers; the remainder has the sign of the dividend (or is 0) and `|a % b| < |b|`, so `%` never overflows; `a == (a / b) * b + a % b` holds where `a / b` does not panic. Not floored modulo (`-7 % 3` is `-1`). No `wrapping_rem`/`checked_rem`/`rem_euclid` (YAGNI). Remainder by zero left to its own ticket. §3.6 *Integer Division*, §3.2 *Overflow behavior* | Q1 6-0 A; Q2 6-0 S-in; Phase B skipped (one option after dedupe), no Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Integer remainder by zero | **`/` and `%` panic on a zero divisor (Q1)**: on every integer type, `a / b` and `a % b` panic when `b` is 0; the message contains `division by zero` for both operators, and the rest of the message and the source location are not specified. **Constant zero divisor deferred (Q2)**: whether a divisor known at compile time to be 0 is a compile error goes to its own ticket; §3.2 covers overflow, and a zero divisor is not overflow. **Back-pointer (Q3)**: the `%` definition reads "for a non-zero `b` (a zero `b` panics, as above)". **Example (Q4)**: `let m = 7 % z       // RUNTIME PANIC: Int division by zero`, in the format of the overflow line next to it. No compiler change. §3.6 *Integer Division* | Q1 6-0 A; Q2 6-0 D (defer); Q3 5-1 YES (Min dissent); Q4 4-1-1 E1, unchanged after Phase D (AI/ML E2, Min E0); AI-First 5/5 pass |
