| Lexer error and `blink.toml` warning codes | **Lexer codes E1114–E1126** (§2.4): one code per malformed-escape kind (EmptyUnicodeEscape … UnicodeEscapeNeedsBraces E1120); `\x..` → HexEscapeNotSupported E1121 (Q1); `"\0"` and `"\u{0}"` → NulInStr E1122 (Q3); UnknownEscape E1123 for both literal kinds, with one rule for targeted help: where the escape has a known meaning in another language, the help gives the Blink fix first (Q11); EmptyCharLiteral E1124 + MultiCharLiteral E1125 (Q2); one UnterminatedLiteral E1126 for every unclosed literal, owned by the lexer, so E1100 no longer fires at EOF (Q4); span on the opening delimiter + `note:` where it ran out (Q5). **Recovery (Q10)**: no setting suppresses a lexer error; the lexer emits the token, keeps lexing, reports every error, and recovery causes no follow-on diagnostic. **Manifest (§8.9.8)**: unknown top-level section → warning UnknownManifestSection W1001 with did-you-mean, promotable (Q6); E1200 and E1052 add a note pointing at it (Q7); unknown `[lints]` level → InvalidLintLevel E0843, refused, not ignored (Q8); every manifest diagnostic spans the value, key or header, with no fallback (Q9). See [rationale](decisions/lexer-and-manifest-diagnostic-codes.md) | Q1, Q3, Q5, Q6, Q8, Q9, Q12 6-0; Q7 5-1 (Min); Q10 5-1 (Min); Q13 5-1 (PLT); Q2 4-1-1 then 6-0 after Phase D; Q11 4-2 then 6-0 after Phase D; Q4 3-3 then 4-2 after Phase D (PLT, AI/ML dissent); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Contracts on trait methods | **Inherit, promise more, never demand more (Q1)**: a trait method may carry `@requires`/`@ensures`; every impl inherits them; an impl `@requires` is E0912 `TraitImplAddsPrecondition` (note: already inherited; help: move it to the trait, a refinement type in the trait's signature, or a free fn); an impl may add `@ensures`, ANDed with the trait's; a restated inherited `@ensures` is W0900; callers through `T: Trait` assume only the trait's `@ensures`, callers on the concrete type assume both. **`@verify` (Q2)**: a trait method may carry `@verify(fallback: "runtime")` as the default for every impl body with no `@verify`; `"trust"` there is E0913 `TrustOnTraitMethod`; an impl's own `@verify` replaces the default as a whole; it resolves at elaboration and covers default and override bodies; a fn's own `@verify` governs its whole contract and a runtime `@requires` check runs at entry. **Binding (Q3)**: inherited predicates bind by position, `self` at position 0; no name check; every surface prints both names; no rename lint (W0850 rejected). **Q4**: no contract change by subtrait redeclaration (E0733); default bodies verified once over `Self`; mono never brings in an impl contract; predicate scope = params, `self`, `result`, `old()`, methods of the trait, supertraits and bounds; no `@ffi` fn implements a contracted method (E0803); a contract predicate of `f` may not call `f`, directly or through a cycle (E1309); no formatter text; E1110 for trait-method contracts removed. See §3b.2 *Contracts on Trait Methods* | Q1 6-0 (sub-point 5-1, Min dissent); Q2 6-0 R2 after 3-3 R1; Q3 6-0 (W0850 lint rejected 6-0 R2 after 3-3 R1); Q4 6-0 except 4-7 5-1 (DevOps dissent) |
| Str byte-range slice and out-of-range indices | **Byte API (Q0)**: add `byte_slice(start, end) -> Str`, the byte range `[start, end)`; `Str.slice` and `substr` are removed; `index_of -> Option[Int]` stays a codepoint index; `byte_slice` panics when a bound falls inside a multi-byte codepoint (no snapping); `len` marked O(n) in the table; no `Option`-returning range forms; nominal `ByteOffset`/`CharIndex` types **deferred, not rejected**. **Range reads clamp (Q1)**: `substring`, `byte_slice`, `List.slice`, `Bytes.slice` share one rule: `start < 0` → 0, `end` past length → length, `start >= end` → empty, no swap, no count from the end. **Point reads (Q2, Q3)**: `byte_at` keeps `-> U8` and panics out of bounds (message names method, index, `byte_len`); general rule "a point read never makes up a value: `Option` when the return type can say absent, otherwise panic". **`byte_index_of(needle, from)` (Q4)**: `from` required; `from < 0` → 0, `from` past `byte_len` → `None`, `from` inside a codepoint panics; every `Some(i)` is a valid `byte_slice` bound. **Closed set (Q5)**: the spec states the `byte_` methods are exactly `byte_len`, `byte_at`, `byte_slice`, `byte_index_of`. **UTF-8 (Q6)**: every safe `Str` constructor gives valid UTF-8; `@ffi`/`@trusted` code carries the obligation. **Migration order (Q7)**: `substring` must never carry both meanings in one tree; byte callers move to `byte_slice` before `substring`/`len`/`char_at`/`index_of` flip to codepoints. **Unit-mix lint (Q8)**: follow-up ticket. §3.2.1 *Out-of-range indices*, §3.2.2 *Why `.get()` returns `Option[T]`* | Q0–Q3, Q7 6-0; Q4 6-0 in Phase D after 3-3 in Phase C; Q5 4-2 in Phase D after 3-3 in Phase C (PLT, Min: DECISIONS.md only); Q6 5-1 (Min: ticket only); Q8 5-1 (Min: no ticket yet); AI-First 4/5 pass (Debuggability: clamp and unit mix give no signal) |
| `Rand` operations and `std.random` | **Ops (Q1)**: `Rand` has `fn int(lo: Int, hi: Int) -> Int`, `fn float() -> Float`, `fn bytes(n: Int) -> Bytes`; `int` the closed interval `[lo, hi]`, `float` in `[0.0, 1.0)`; user code calls only wrappers, `rand.int(r: Range[Int])` normalizes `..`/`..=` and panics on an empty range (`rand.int: empty range 5..5`); every handler must be exactly uniform, no modulo bias, no algorithm named (Q1-U). **Results checked (Q6)**: each wrapper checks every result in every build mode and panics `Rand handler contract violated: int(0, 4) returned 9`. **Helpers (Q2, Q3)**: free functions `shuffle` (new list, Fisher-Yates, exactly `n - 1` draws) and `choice` (`Option[T]`, `None` on empty) in `std.random`, nothing else; no `random_int`/`random_float`/`random_range`. **Resolution (Q2-R)**: `rand.<name>` always means the handle; `rand.shuffle` is E0505 with a note listing the ops and a fix to import `std.random.{shuffle}`. **Fix-it (Q7d)**: `rand.int(0, 10)` and `rand.int(10)` get the fix `rand.int(0..10)` and a note that `0..=10` includes 10. **Seeding (P1, Q4)**: no seed/reseed/fork op; seeding is installing a handler; no production seeded handler now (a later one must name its algorithm and freeze its stream). **Secrets (Q5, P6)**: one effect; the default handler is a CSPRNG described by properties only (unpredictable, OS-entropy seeded, distinct per thread and after fork) and is fit for secrets, but a `with` handler replaces it. **Rejected**: `PartialRandHandler` warning (Q7a), single `u64()` op (Q7b), compile error for a literal empty range (Q7c) | 6-0 Q1 1b, 6-0 Q1-U, 6-0 Q2 2a R2 (was 4-2 R1; web/devops 2c), 6-0 Q2-R, 6-0 Q3 3a, 5-1 Q4 defer (web dissent: seeded handler with a stable stream), 6-0 Q5 5a, 6-0 Q6 6a, 5-1 Q7a no R2 (was 4-2 yes R1; devops dissent), 6-0 Q7b no, 6-0 Q7c no R2 (was 4-2 yes R1), 6-0 Q7d yes, 6-0 P6 properties only |
| `todo()` and `unreachable()` built-ins | **Built-ins (Q1)**: `todo` and `unreachable`, each `-> Never` with two forms (`f()` and `f(msg: Str)`, Q5: no positional-default notation, builtin-only); at run time each is a `panic`; no `unimplemented`. **`unreachable` is a checked panic in every build mode, never undefined behavior**, and is only for paths the author states cannot run (`Result` for errors, `todo` for unfinished code). **Message (Q4, Q7)**: optional; exact text left to the panic-message ticket, but `todo` text contains `not yet implemented` and `unreachable` text contains `entered unreachable code`, stable for `assert_panics(matching:)`. **Warning (Q2)**: `TodoInCode` (W0703) at every call that resolves to the built-in `todo`, test blocks included; in a test the help names `@skip`. `unreachable` has no warning. **Library-panic lint (Q3a, Q3b)**: neither built-in triggers it (keyed on resolution to the built-in, not the name); its help never proposes a plain `panic`→`unreachable` rename; its name and code go to a separate ticket. **Divergence (Q6)**: §3.3 replaces its list of names with "a call whose result type is `Never`", which covers user `-> Never` fns; inference still never chooses `Never`. §2.20, §3.3, ERROR_CATALOG.md | 5-1 Q1 TU R2 (was 4-2 R1; web changed T→TU, min dissent: T, add `unreachable` when the lint needs it), 6-0 Q2 all sites, 6-0 Q3a exempt, 5-1 Q3b later (aiml dissent: name it now), 5-1 Q4 optional (min dissent: required), 5-1 Q5 two forms (devops dissent: `assert` notation), 6-0 Q6 by type, 5-1 Q7 stable substring (min dissent: defer all text); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| `Never`, the bottom type | Removes a self-contradiction: §2.20 said `Never` "inhabits every type" and §3.7 said no such type exists. **Core (Q1)**: `Never` is a nameable prelude built-in with no values, writable in any type position (return, parameter, field, `let`, type argument); a subtype of every type; inference never chooses it; "inhabits" is gone from the spec; §3.7 forbids a *value* that belongs to every type. **One definition in §3.2 (Q2)**, with §3.7 text "`Never` (§3.2) is the bottom type, but it has no values" (Q3). **No introduction form (Q4)**: only a construct that diverges has type `Never`. **Fits wherever any type is expected (Q5)**, but **no lifting (Q8)**: `List[Never]` is not `List[Int]`, `Result[Int, Never]` is not `Result[Int, Str]`, `fn() -> Never` is not `fn() -> Int`; widen with `match r { Ok(v) => Ok(v), Err(e) => e }` in a checked position (Q9); an E0300 help names that rewrite first, `map_err` with a written target type second. **Arm required (Q7)**: no pruning of `Never`-payload arms. **Effects (Q6)**: `-> Never` keeps the effect row; §4.4.3 states that a handler fn for any op declared `-> Never` cannot return and must diverge. **Traits (Q10)**: no impls unless the prelude declares one; no ex-falso blanket impls; which impls is a separate ticket. **E0311 (Q11)**: for `-> Never` the help names only a diverging tail, never `Option[R]`. **W0700 after `Never` (Q12)**: deferred. §2.11, §2.20, §3.2, §3.3, §3.5, §3.7, §4.4.3, §7 prelude table; catalog E0311 | Q1, Q2, Q4, Q7, Q8, Q10–Q12 6-0; Q3 5-1 in Phase D (web: drop "bottom type"); Q5 5-1 in Phase D (min: sentence only in the match note); Q6 6-0 and Q9 6-0 in Phase D (both 4-2 in Phase C); user signed off; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Shadowing a prelude trait (`trait Eq` and `==`, `@derive(Eq)`) | **Written names by scope, inserted forms by identity (Q1)**: in a module that declares a prelude trait name, a bound `T: Eq`, `impl Eq for`, a supertrait clause and the `@derive` argument name the local trait; the operators, the prelude declarations' own bounds (`Ord: Eq`, `Hash: Eq`, `Map`/`Set` keys, `assert_eq`, container equality) and the `Eq` that `@derive(Ord)` derives use the compiler-known trait by identity; `import blink.core.{Eq as CoreEq}` is the only escape, no path form; Option 2 (reserve `Eq`/`Ord`/`Hash`) rejected. **New code (Q2)**: a `@derive` argument that is not a derivable compiler-known trait by identity is E1402 `NotDerivable` (local shadow or any user trait), help gives the aliased import and `@derive(CoreEq)`; E1401 keeps its field-type meaning; after the vote the compiler's undocumented E1112 `UnknownDerive` was found, and the user ruled the spec keeps E1402 (E1112 goes in implementation). **W1010 note (Q3)**: on every shadowed prelude trait that a desugaring uses, one note names the forms that still use the builtin; content fixed, words open; the list comes from §3.4 *Hygiene* and the *Prelude Traits* table; `@derive` never listed. **Module scope (Q4)**: a shadow changes lookup only in the declaring module and modules that import the declaration; the compiler's program-wide removal of the prelude trait is a separate bug (identity-keyed trait table, module-qualified C symbols as acceptance criteria). §3.4 *Hygiene*, §3.6.1, §10.6 *Shadowing a Prelude Trait*, catalog E1402. See [rationale](decisions/prelude-trait-shadowing.md) | Q1, Q2, Q4 6-0; Q3 5-1 (Min dissent: `Eq`/`Ord`/`Hash` only); user signed off; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Float `ieee_eq` and `-0.0` | **Meaning (Q1)**: `x.ieee_eq(y)` is IEEE 754 compareQuietEqual (C `==`): `NaN` is unequal to every value, itself included, and `(-0.0).ieee_eq(0.0)` is `true`; the old text "`-0.0 != 0.0`" was wrong, and a sign-aware `ieee_eq` (ticket option b) is rejected. **Law (Q2)**: `x == y` is `true` exactly when `x.ieee_eq(y)` is `true` or both operands are `NaN`; it replaces "differs only on NaN". **Negative rule (Q4)**: no `Float` equality tells `-0.0` from `0.0`. **Sign of zero (Q3)**: the spec gives `1.0 / x < 0.0`; a later `to_bits()`/`is_sign_negative()` must replace that line. **Naming (Q5)**: "the method", no built-in or stdlib claim. **Not filed**: lint `x.ieee_eq(x)` → `x.is_nan()` (Q6); moving `ieee_eq` into std.float (Q7). No compiler change. §3 *Float Total Ordering*. See [rationale](decisions/float-ieee-eq.md) | Q1, Q2, Q4, Q5, Q7 6-0; Q3 5-1 (Min dissent: no sentence); Q6 6-0 in Phase D (4-2 in Phase C); user signed off; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| BlockHandler state between `enter` and `exit` | **No trait change (Q1)**: `self` is by value, so a `BlockHandler` gets all its state when it is built; build it with a factory call in the `with` item, which runs right before `enter()`, so each entry gets a new value and nesting is correct. The §4.6.3 Timer becomes `timer(label)` with `fn enter(self) -> () {}`, and the "do not copy" note goes. **Arena (Q1-arena)**: §5.5 says `with arena { }` behaves as a `BlockHandler` as if the compiler builds a new scope value at each `with arena`. **W0611 `ScopedValueEnteredTwice` (Q2)**: a scoped value that reaches more than one `with`, including a `with` in a loop on a value built outside it; same flow analysis as W0610, own code and severity; does not follow a value into a call. **Second `exit` (Q3)**: stdlib native handles return a defined error after close; user `exit` gets "should" guidance only. **Q5**: UnusedVariable must not fire on a trait-required `self`; that fix lands before or with the example. **Q6**: ParamAssign help in `BlockHandler.enter()` names the factory pattern. **Future (Q4)**: a state slot, if added, is separate from `Context` and seen only by `exit` (not `exit(self, ctx, ok)`); recommended `type State` with `enter -> (Self.Context, Self.State)`, order and signature open to the fallible-enter panel; lands in one trait change with fallible `enter`; W0611 then narrows to `State == ()`; recorded in decisions/ only. §4.6.3, §5.5, §3.6, ERROR_CATALOG.md | Q1 4-2 after 4-2 R1 (Sys, PLT dissent for a `State` slot now); Q1-arena 6-0; Q2 6-0 R2 after 4-1-1 R1, wording 4-2 (Sys, PLT for "state fixed at build time"); Q3 6-0; Q4 6-0 R2 after 4-2 R1; Q5 6-0; Q6 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Test file outcomes + test line stream | `blink test --json` is NDJSON with a `record` key: `test` (one per test, written as it ends, carries `file`), `file` (one per file), `run` (last); a consumer ignores an unknown `record` value. Each `file` line has a closed `outcome: ran \| build_failed \| aborted` and the six-key `summary` (all zero for `build_failed`). `build_failed` carries §8.6 `diagnostics` (never empty, no `span` without a location); a back-end failure is `ice` for generated C and `error` for link/native-dependency steps, full tool output in `notes`. `aborted` = ended by a signal or exited before the end-of-run report (`exit(0)` too); flat `signal` name (`"SIG<n>"` fallback) or `exit_code`; optional `running`, never a guess. Finished test lines stay and count; `files: {total, ran, build_failed, aborted}`; `files.aborted > 0` makes counts a lower bound. Exit 1 when `failed + panicked + files.build_failed + files.aborted > 0`; a file that does not build never stops the run. No new status or cause; a signal is never `panicked`. Human word `ABORTED` | 6-0 core bundle, completion marker, lower bound; Q1 per-test lines 3-3 → 6-0 R2; unknown `record` 6-0; signal name 3-3 → 6-0 R2, flat keys **user (BDFL) tiebreak** after 3-3 R2; severity split by step 4-2 R2 (Sys, DevOps dissent → `error` for every step), output in `notes` by user decision; zero summary 5-1 R2 (Min dissent); `running` 5-1 (Min dissent → none); `ABORTED` 5-1 (Web dissent → `CRASHED`/`EXITED`) |
| Opaque types and type-scoped declarations | **Opacity (Q1)**: `pub opaque type T { ... }` exports the name only; outside the declaring module no literal, spread, variant, field read/write or pattern (`T { .. }` included, V1); an opaque enum hides all variants (V2); `opaque` is a contextual word between `pub` and `type` (V3); no per-field `pub`. Checked against the module of the source text, not the mono site (C1b); an alias does not open the type (C1c); `==`/`<` on an opaque type in a const expression outside the module is an error (C1d); `opaque` changes visibility, not value semantics, and is not `@ffi.opaque` (C1a, no C layout text in sections/). Violations reuse E1003; the message names the type and module, never the field (V4). Derived `Debug` prints hidden fields; informative note only (V5). **Constants (Q2)**: top-level `pub const T.NAME = <const expr>` in the module that declares `T`; a const reference in §2.21; annotation rule of a plain `const` (V6); none on generic types for now (V8); the prelude owns builtin-type constants (V9). **Functions (Q3)**: `pub fn T.name(...)`, no receiver, never reached by method syntax; an explicit amendment to §3c *No Inherent Methods*; replaces the hidden stdlib `Type_name` convention. **One `T.` namespace (V7)**: a clash is E1016, package-wide; with a trait impl from another package it is E0522 at the use site. **Combined flags (V10)**: out of scope; §2.21 states that an operator on a user type is a trait call and not admitted (no "never"). §2.12.2, §2.12.3, §2.21, §2.23, §3c.4, §3 Instant, §9.1.3.4 MsgFlags. | Method scope: impls of the type's package go with the value | **Rule (Q1)**: for `x.foo()` with `x: T`, an impl in `T`'s package is a candidate wherever its trait is visible, with no import; any other impl needs its trait in scope (declared, selectively imported, prelude, or a bound). A module that another file or a dependency loads adds no candidates. Replaces the old §3.6 "trait or type in scope" rule. **Home = package (Q2)**, the orphan-rule unit; the set comes from the package contents, never the load graph. **Whole-module import (Q3)**: `import ext` does not put `ext`'s traits in method-call scope; `import ext.{Extra}` does. **Bounds and visibility (Q4)**: a bound counts as in scope; the trait must be visible at the call site, home impls included. **Hint (Q5)**: E0505 must name the trait and give the import line for an impl in `T`'s package or a declared dependency, and for `import ext` it says the whole-module import does not enable method calls and rewrites it to `import ext.{Extra}`; the spec fixes the outcome, not the search; the list may be ranked and capped. **Lint (Q6)**: a type imported only for its methods is W0602 (unused import); §8.10.3 examples drop `MockClock`/`MockEnv`. E0522 names each owner's full path and the import that brought it in. | `Bytes` aliasing: value, not shared cell | **`Bytes` is a value (Q1)**: not on the §3.6 shared-cell list; a write through one name never shows through another (bind, pass, return, `clone()`, field, template hole); how codegen avoids sharing is not spec text. **Writers take `mut self` (Q2)**: `set`, `set_*`, `push`, `write_*_le/be`, `with_mut_ptr`; a call on an immutable root is E0610. **Complete method table (Q3a)**: §3.2.3 lists every shipped method (`new`, `push`, `set`, `write_*`) and no unshipped one. **E0814 follows the table (Q3b)**: a call to a method the table marks *grows* (`push`, `write_*`) inside a pin; the hand list (`append`, `concat`, `extend`, `clear`, `truncate`, `resize`, `@bytes_grows`) is gone. **Two pins (Q4)**: `with_ptr` (plain `self`, read-only; C must not write through `p`) and `with_mut_ptr` (`mut self`, C may write); E0814/E0815 cover both; libc read wrappers use `let mut buf` + `with_mut_ptr`. **No rollout text in the spec (Q5)**: value codegen with its aliasing tests lands with or before E0610 (binding on the impl ticket). **No cost text in sections/ (Q6)**: the cost promises live in the rationale. | `List` capacity | **`List.with_capacity(n)` (Q1)**: static constructor, returns an empty list (`len()` 0), `n` is a hint; use only when the final size is known. **No `reserve` (Q2)**: deferred. **`capacity()` (Q3)**: public, read-only, exactly three rules: `capacity() >= len()`; `capacity() >= n` right after `with_capacity(n)`; `clear()` does not lower it; growth factor unspecified; tests and diagnostics only, do not branch on it. **`n <= 0` (Q4)**: same as `new()`, no panic. **`Map`/`Set` (Q5)**: deferred; same name and shape if added. **One rule (Q6)**: every `with_capacity` constructor (`List`, `StringBuilder`) follows the same `n` rules. | Enum equality: fieldless implicit, data enums derive | **Rule (Q1)**: a fieldless enum is `Eq` by variant with no derive; an enum with at least one payload is `Eq` only through `@derive(Eq)` or a written `impl Eq`, the same as a struct; payload types alone never make an enum `Eq` (replaces the interim rule from Container Equality Q9). **`impl Eq` on a fieldless enum (Q2)**: compile error, because it could split `==` from the variant, the `Int` tag comparison and `@derive(Hash)`. **Redundant derive (Q3)**: `@derive(Eq)` on a fieldless enum is legal, no warning, still checked. **Recursion (Q4)**: `@derive(Eq)`/`impl Eq` on a recursive type resolve coinductively, also for mutual recursion across modules and generic `Tree[T]` with the inferred `T: Eq`. **Migration (Q5)**: immediate error with a machine-applicable `@derive(Eq)` fix at the declaration; release notes name `blink fix`; measured cost 24 test sites, 0 in src/ and lib/. `Ord` for enums is a separate ticket. §3.6 *Container Equality*, §3.6 *Hash Contract and Seeding*. Built-in-effect "not supported yet" case keeps its code. §4.7.1; ERROR_CATALOG.md E0544. | `default` in a closure in a handler operation | **Closures (Q1)**: `default` is rejected in every closure, escaping or not; a closure body is not an operation body, even when written inside one. **Code (Q2)**: E0544 `DefaultOutsideHandlerOp` (no new code; E0505 no longer covers it); the message names the closure and the help gives the fix. **Own effect in an op body (Q3)**: while an operation body runs, its handler is not in force for the effect it handles; every handle call made while the body runs (direct, from a closure the body calls, or from a function the body calls) reaches the handler this one was installed over, so `audit.op(...)` and `default.op(...)` do the same thing there; the handler is in force again when the operation returns or panics; a closure that runs later uses the handlers where it is called (§4.15.4). **Lint (Q4)**: none. §4.7.1; ERROR_CATALOG.md E0544. See [rationale](decisions/default-in-closure-in-handler-op.md) | Q1 5-1 (plt: lexical capture); Q2 6-0; Q3 6-0; Q4 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass; user-approved |
| Shared cells in a spawned task | **Spawn (Q1)**: from `async.spawn` until the spawner joins the task (`h.await` or `async.scope` exit), each shared cell (§3.6) the closure's captures reach, at any depth, is read-only; a write through any name, from any task, panics; reads unchanged; the panic names the write site, the spawn site and the captured binding. **Lint (Q2)**: `warning[SpawnCaptureMutAlias]` on a write through a direct `let mut` alias of a captured `let` after the spawn and before the join; not complete, the freeze is the guarantee. **Channels (Q3)**: `ch.send` copies each shared cell the value reaches; the receiver gets its own. **E0650 help (Q4)**: binding a List does not copy it; compute inside the task or use a channel; `let snapshot = count` for scalars; `xs.slice(0, xs.len())` one level only. Handler state at the spawn site not yet decided (later decided: homed handlers, see *Channel, `Handle[T]` and handlers in a spawned task*). §2.8, §3.6, §4.13, §4.15.4 | Q1 **4-2** (web, aiml: copy at spawn) and Q2 **4-2** (sys, devops: error) after Phase D; Q3 5-1 (min: defer); Q4 5-1 (devops: await-first help); AI-First 4/5 (Consistency fails: freeze at spawn, copy at send) |
| With-ptr closure and FFI regions | **Region (Q1)**: no fourth FFI region; a `Bytes.with_ptr` / `with_mut_ptr` closure is an ordinary closure literal and inherits the region it is written in, or has none; outside a region its `Ptr[U8]` parameter is E0811, named or `_`, so either pin is legal only inside a region; the five `std.libc` `*_bytes` wrappers become `@trusted(audit: K)` `pub fn`s. **One report (Q2)**: E0811 reports once, at the outermost expression (by the syntax tree) whose type holds a `Ptr`; a site that gets its pointer from a reported site is not reported again; a separate source reports on its own. **Table (Q3)**: no `with_ptr` row in the pointer-operation table. **Help (Q4)**: no-pointer fixes (`*_bytes` wrapper, `b.get(i)` under a conservative syntactic test) before `@trusted`; never `with ffi.scope()`; neither line machine-applicable; the spec fixes order and limits, not words. §9.1.1, §9.1.3, §3.2.3 | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0 (sys, web from R-CALL; devops from R-TOOLS); Q3 5-1 (plt: non-normative row); Q4 **6-0** after Phase D (3-3 in Phase C: sys, plt, min for leaving help text to the implementation); AI-First 5/5 |
| `@trusted` channel for the scoped-value lints | **Ordinary lints (Q1)**: W0610 `ScopedValueWithoutWith` and W0611 `ScopedValueEnteredTwice` are warnings that `[lints]` sets per project and `@allow(Name)` suppresses per function (§4.16.8); `@trusted(audit: K)` does not reach them, and they are not on the §9.1 audit-gated list. **Closed list (Q2)**: §9.1 states that `@trusted(audit: K)` acts only on the listed diagnostics (and its §9.1.1 region role); a new member must meet the membership criterion. **Pointer (Q3)**: §5.5 says "`@trusted(audit: K)` does not suppress it (§9.1)" for each lint. **Note (Q4)**: when either lint fires in a function with `@trusted`, the output adds a note that `@trusted` does not suppress it and names `@allow`; no new code. **Help (Q5)**: the note reads "if this function manages the lifetime by hand, add `@allow(ScopedValueWithoutWith)`" (W0611: "if running `exit()` more than once is intended"). **LSP (Q6)**: the `@allow` fix comes after the `with` fix and is never machine-applicable | Q1 A 6-0, Q2 R 6-0 (PLT moved P→R), Q3 M 5-1 (Minimalism dissent: no pointer), Q4 6-0, Q5 C 6-0, Q6 6-0 |
| Power-assert sub-expression values | **Debug rendering (Q1)**: each `assert(expr)` value line is `<source text> = <debug(value)>`; strings quoted. **Omit at typecheck (Q2)**: an operand whose static type, as written, has no `Debug` under the bounds in scope gets no line; never decided per instantiation (`fn f[T: Eq]` at `T = Int` prints no line); no placeholder, no error. **Note line (Q3)**: one runtime `note:` per omitted operand, after the value lines, before `-->`, text fixed per site. **Three note forms (Q3a)**: a struct or enum the asserting package defines (orphan-rule test) → `<Type> has no Debug (add @derive(Debug))`; a type parameter → `<T> has no Debug bound`; any other type (foreign, function, FFI, container with a non-Debug element) → `<Type> has no Debug`. **JSON (Q3b, Q6)**: `introspection` (`{"expr","value"}`) and `omitted` (`{"expr","type"}`) always present on the record for a failed `assert*` call, `[]` when empty. **Closed shape table (Q4)**: comparison operands; method receiver and each argument; free-call arguments; `!e` as `e`; top-level `&&`/`\|\|` one Bool line per operand that ran; no arithmetic row. **`mut` operands (Q4a)**: an operand bound to a `mut` parameter gets no line and is not rebound, decided from the callee signature. **No text dedupe (Q4b)**. **Closed literal list skips its line (Q5)**; composites keep it. **Fixed set (Q7)**: lines are decided at compile time from shape and static types, except a `&&`/`\|\|` operand that did not run. §2.20 *Sub-expression Values* | 6-0 Q1, Q2, Q3b, Q4a, Q4b, Q5, Q6, Q7; Q3 5-1 (Min: a note is a stand-in one level up); Q4 5-1 (Min: S-cmp+logic); **Q3a 6-0 R2** after 3-3 R1; **hint wording 6-0** (short form) after 3-3; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Map Debug entry order | **Order (Q1)**: `Map[K,V].debug()` sorts entries by the UTF-8 bytes of `key.debug()`, then of `value.debug()` when two keys render the same text; not iteration order; no `Ord` bound. **Law (Q2)**: for a type that is `Eq` and `Debug`, `a == b` must give `a.debug() == b.debug()`; an unchecked contract like `Hash`/`Eq`; derived `Debug` obeys it when `Eq` is also derived; a hand `impl` of either must keep them in agreement. **Float (Q3)**: the one built-in exception: `-0.0 == 0.0` but they may render differently; Float output unchanged. *Superseded by Float text form: no built-in exception remains.* **Iteration (Q4)**: `keys()`, `values()` and `for` keep iteration order; only `debug()` sorts. **Example (Q5)**: `{1: "a", 10: "c", 2: "b"}` beside Set `{1, 10, 2}`. **Layout (Q6)**: one shared *Set and Map order* paragraph. §3.6.1 *Debug vs Display* / *Container Debug Rendering* | 6-0 Q1–Q6; no Phase D (unanimous); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Channel, `Handle[T]` and handlers in a spawned task | **Category (Q1)**: `Channel[T]` and `Handle[T]` are *synchronized objects*, a list in §3.6 beside shared cells: any task may use one with no freeze and no copy; a copy names the same queue or task; spawn does not freeze one and `ch.send` does not copy one. Entry condition: each operation is safe from any task, and a value handed between tasks stays reachable from one task only. Soundness: a value in a channel's buffer was copied at send, so no other name reaches it. Law: "Only a shared cell or a synchronized object can change through a second name." Effect handle names (`io`, `db`) are not values (§3c.4). **Name (Q1-name)**: "synchronized object". **`Handle[T]` (Q2)**: decided here; a second `.await`, on the handle or a copy, returns the same result (its cells shared, as with a bind); a `Handle` that a `ch.send` value or a spawn capture reaches is a compile error where the type shows it (generic case shows the instantiation chain) and a panic otherwise (naming the send or capture site and the handle's spawn site); the compile check is not complete. **Homed handlers (Q3)**: a handler in force at an `async.spawn` site whose captures reach a shared cell runs its ops on the task that ran its `with`; an op from another task waits until the home task runs it, which it does whenever it blocks in a runtime blocking operation (`.await`, `recv`, `send`, scope exit, `time.sleep`, blocking I/O), not in an `@ffi` call. **Re-entrancy (C2)**: while an op of a homed handler runs on the home task, that handler's waiting ops do not run; other homed handlers' ops may. **Cycle (D)**: an op body that waits by `.await` or scope exit on a task that (directly or through tasks it awaits) has an op of the same handler waiting panics, naming the `with` site, the op and the spawn site; a cycle through a channel is a stated deadlock and is not always detected. §2.8, §3.6, §3.6.1, §4.13, §4.15.4 | 6-0 Q1, Q1-name, Q2-scope, Q2-await, Q2-cross, Q3, re-entrancy; blocking I/O in the servicing list **6-0 keep** in Phase D (min, devops from narrow); **await-cycle 3-3 in Phase D → user broke tie for a required panic** (plt, min, aiml: stated deadlock); AI-First 5/5 pass; user-approved |
| Float text form | **Digits**: fewest significant digits that convert back to the same Float, then nearest the exact value, then even last digit; the spec fixes output, not algorithm. **Zero (Q1)**: `-0.0` renders `0.0`; the §3.6.1 Float exception to the Debug/Eq law is deleted (replaces Map Debug Q3). **Layout (Q2, Q3)**: plain form when −4 ≤ k < 16, else exponent form with lowercase `e`, no `+`, no leading zeros; every finite output contains `.` (`1.0`, `1.0e16`, `1.0e-5`). **Specials (Q4, Q5)**: every NaN `NaN`; `inf` / `-inf`. **Laws (Q1, Q12)**: `x.display().parse_float() == Ok(x)` under Blink `==`; `x == y` exactly when `x.display() == y.display()`. **F32 (Q7)**: any F32 text uses binary32 shortest digits; no Display impl added. **parse_float (Q8)**: must accept every display output and round correctly; rest of the input grammar is a separate ticket. *Superseded by `parse_float` / `parse_int` input grammar.* **Literals (Q9)**: exponent float literals are a separate ticket. **Scope (Q10)**: every place Blink, its stdlib or the `blink` toolchain writes a finite Float as decimal text uses this form unless the spec names another; JSON NaN/inf belongs to §3.6.2. **Locale (Q11)**: decimal mark always `.`. §3.2.1 *Parsing*, §3.6 *Float Text Form*, §3.6.1 *Debug vs Display* | 6-0 Q2–Q5, Q7, Q9, Q11, Q12; **5-1 Q1** (Systems dissent: keep `-0.0`); **5-1 Q8** (DevOps dissent: state a minimum input grammar); Q10 4-1-1 in Phase C → **6-0 in Phase D**, wording 4-2; AI-First 5/5 pass; user-approved |
| In-context language reference | **Source (Q1)**: the core is marked blocks in `sections/` (`<!-- core:<id> -->`, stable semantic IDs); a generator builds `llms-full.md`; `sections/` is the only normative source. **Caps (Q2, Q2b)**: core ≤ 46,000 bytes, hard fail, raised only by panel vote; topic ≤ 12 KiB; index ≤ 6 KiB. **API (Q3)**: core holds prelude type/trait names plus one line per prelude trait; method tables generated from lib/std as topics; an API-shape divergence is a core block whose fence pins the shape. **Scope (Q4)**: install, flags, emit modes, daemon, FFI, arena are topics with one pointer line each; CLI topics generated from CLI registrations. **Checks (Q5)**: caps, fences compile, error fences fail with the stated code, regen-diff, unique and resolving IDs, closure over explicit references, diagnostic codes in catalog. **Order (Q6)**: divergence list at head, common-errors list at tail, each bullet cites a core ID. **CLI (Q7)**: `blink llms --full` = core; `--all`; `--list` shows sizes. **Eval (Q8)**: out; separate release chore. **Landing (Q9)**: cap the hand-trimmed file first. **Labels (Q10)**: no "*Rationale (normative)*". §8.14, §8.17 | 6-0 except Q2b 5-1 (Min: no topic cap yet); Q3 3-3 → 6-0, Q5 4-1-1 → 6-0, Q8 4-2 → 6-0 in Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Unknown contracts default to a runtime check | **Default (Q1)**: an Unknown `@requires`/`@ensures` gets a runtime check (`@requires` at entry, `@ensures` at each exit with `old()` snapshots); a failure panics, or raises `validation.contract_violation` at a system boundary; V0003 is no longer the default. **Builds (Q2)**: the check runs in every build mode; no flag, profile or `blink.toml` setting removes it; `"trust"` on one fn is the only opt-out. **Strict value (Q3, Q3b)**: new `@verify(fallback: "error")` makes Unknown V0003; a trait method may carry it as the impl default (this widens *Contracts on trait methods* Q2, which allowed `"runtime"` only); adding it to a published trait is a breaking change. **`"runtime"` (Q4)**: stays legal; W0901 `RedundantVerifyFallback` + autofix where redundant, quiet where it replaces a trait `"error"`. **Scope (Q5)**: no project or module default; CI uses `blink verify --deny runtime,trust`. **Diagnostics (Q6)**: info V0004 `ContractRuntimeChecked` (silent, source default/explicit/inherited); report shows cost class and `old()` snapshots; spec fixes the failure message (contract, clause kind, fn, span, capped free-variable values, caller) built on a cold path; V0003 says when no solver ran and offers "simplify" only when one ran; `@verify` with no contract is W0902 `VerifyWithoutContract`; report marks a proof that assumes a callee `"trust"`. **Executable predicates (Q7)**: the runtime default applies to an executable predicate; a non-executable one that is Unknown is V0003 unless it has `"trust"`. See §3b.4 | Q1–Q5, Q3b 6-0; Q6a–d, 6f 6-0; Q6e 5-1 (Min dissent: error); Q7 5-1 (Min dissent: rationale only) |
| Effect-tracked builtins | **Rules (Q8)**: no compiler-known free function performs a side effect; every stdlib function and method's row is a sound upper bound (empty = pure, or divergent with result `Never`); an operation that consumes a value a handle op made is a handle op of the same effect; second-tier functions are gated by their row but no handler intercepts them (§4.10). **Removal (Q1)**: the effectful free builtins go, no alias, mapped to `fs.*`, `env.exit`/`env.args()`/`env.pid()`, `process.*`, `time.*`. **Existence (Q2)**: `fs.stat(path) -> Result[FileInfo, FsError]` only; check with a `not_found()` match. **Process (Q3-Q5)**: new §4.4.8 `effect Process { Spawn Signal }`; `run(cmd, args, -- stdin: Option[Str] = None)` (`None` = null device), `spawn`, `wait(child)`, `exec -> Result[Never, ProcessError] ! Process.Spawn, Env.Exit`, `signal(child, sig) ! Process.Signal`; value type `Child`; non-zero exit is data; `ProcessError { op, cmd, code: Errno }`. **Unix sockets (Q6)**: `unix_listen ! Net.Listen` and `unix_connect ! Net.Connect` in `std.net`, second tier; listen never removes an existing path. **Removed names (Q7)**: existing undefined-function error plus a fix from a permanent table, used only when lookup fails. **Clocks (Q9)**: `time.monotonic() -> Monotonic` (opaque, `.since(earlier) -> Duration`); `time.read()` is the wall clock. **Order (Q10)**: handle-op enforcement lands before the migration step. §3.2.3, §4.1, §4.3, §4.4, §4.4.3, §4.4.5, §4.4.6, §4.4.8, §4.10 | 6-0 Q1-Q3, Q4b, Q5, Q6c, Q7-Q10; **Q4a 4-2** (Systems, Web: also allow `child.wait()` method form; follow-up spec ticket); **Q6a 5-1** (Min: compiler-private); **Q6b 4-2** (PLT, DevOps: add `FS.Write`); no Phase D (soft consensus, user-approved) |
| Template literal prefix `t"..."` | **Prefix (Q1)**: `t"..."` makes a template literal; a plain `"..."` is always `Str` and never becomes a `Template[C]`. This replaces the literal coercion named in the `Template[C]` surface row. **All literal forms (Q2)**: `t` precedes `#"..."#` at depths 1–3; W1111 fires inside `t#"..."#`. **Inference (Q3)**: a template literal is `Template[α]`; the expected type or a later use solves α; E0301 at the literal if unsolved, as for `[]`/`None`. **`Raw` is a typing rule (Q4)**: a `Raw[T]` hole of a template literal folds and raises `RawBypassesParam`; a `Raw[T]` anywhere else, including a plain-literal hole, is E0530. **No quoted-hole lint (Q5)**: the std DB handler or a library owns that check. **E0310 reused (Q6)**: the help adds or removes `t` and is machine-applicable; no help offers `Raw`. **Final rule only (Q7)**. **Spec text (Q8)**: every part of a `Template[C]` is literal source text or text folded from a `Raw[T]`; the "call site" sentence is deleted. **Prefix, not delimiter (Q9)**: the "Multiple string delimiters" and "Implicit conversions" rows stand; `t` is a prefix on the one delimiter, as `#` is. It is the only template prefix: no tag for each context (`sql"..."`), and each new prefix needs a panel vote | 6-0 on all 9 Qs |
| Module-level state effect | **Mechanism (Q1)**: one built-in leaf effect `State` (§4.3, §4.16): no operations, no handler (`handler State` / `with` naming it is E0545), held by `main` and test blocks (E0539 never fires for it), ignored by capability budgets, no run-time cost. A `pub fn`, `pub` method or trait impl method that touches module state declares `! State` (E0552, machine-applicable fix); a private function or closure gets `State` in its effective row by inference, and function-value types carry the effective row. **Reads (Q2)**: reads and writes of a module `let mut` both count; immutable `let` and `const` stay free; write sets and W0550/W0551 stay writes-only. Conditions: the E0552 note names each binding, read or written, and the location; W0552 `ModuleLetMutNeverWritten` says "declare it with `let`" (machine-applicable). **Spawn (Q3)**: a function value passed to `async.spawn` whose effective row has `State` is E0651 `SpawnTouchesModuleState`. **Trait impls (Q4)**: E0904 checks the effective row; supersedes `Display.fmt` effect row Q5 for module state. **Text (Q5)**: Principle 3 unchanged and now true; §4.1 exception deleted; `@pure` (E1307) means an empty effective row; user effects are an optional label beside `State`. **Tooling (Q6, not normative)**: `blink query --fn`, `blink doc` and inlay hints show the write set. Supersedes in part *Module-level `let mut` mutation* above. See [rationale](decisions/module-state-effect.md) | Q1 5-1 (DevOps dissent: a per-binding writer effect on every writer); Q2 6-0 R2 after 4-2 R1 (PLT, DevOps for writes only in R1); Q3 5-1 (Web dissent: separate ticket); Q4 6-0; Q5 6-0; Q6 5-1 (Min dissent: leave tooling out) |
| Bare variant and value names | **Model (Q1)**: a variant is not in the value namespace; it lives in `T.`, and the rule is a collision check. **Collision (Q2, Q3)**: a module-scope `let`/`let mut`/`const`/`fn` with the name of a variant of any enum in the same package is E1016. **Prelude (Q5a, Q5c)**: the variants of every prelude enum count as declared in every module, so such a value is E1016 and the message names the prelude type. **Scope order (Q4)**: a bare use finds a local, then a module value or import, then a variant by §3.4; a hint never moves resolution past a binding; a type error at a hinted site gets help naming `T.V`. **W1011 `ShadowedVariant` (Q4w, Q6, Q5b)**: a binding that hides a variant of the same package, of the prelude, or of a type imported through an import entry; a pattern binder warns only when the scrutinee type has no such variant; `@allow(ShadowedVariant)` on the fn. **Patterns (Q7)**: a bare name is a variant only when the scrutinee type has it, else a new binding; no global-unique search in patterns. **Invariant (Q8)**: a bare name at a site denotes exactly one entity, determined statically; `blink query` and the LSP report it. §2.12.1, §3.4, §3.5, §8.2, §10.5, §10.6 Amended by *Qualified variants in expressions*. | 6-0 Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4, Q5a-c, Q7; Q4w 5-1 (Minimalism: no warning); Q6 6-0 R2 after 3-3 R1; Q8 5-1 (Minimalism: implementation note); user approved; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Native dependency keys and link names | **Model (Q1)**: one `[native-dependencies]` entry per dependency, with an optional ordered `libs` list of `-l` names that defaults to `[key]`; `@ffi` names a key only, and E0820 for a name in another entry's `libs` names the owning key. **Rule text (Q2)**: "The key is the library's link name (the `-l` name), unless `libs` is given"; vendored adds no flag, a host pkg-config entry links pkg-config output. **Package field (Q3)**: spec `name` renamed `pkg-config`, required on `type = "pkg-config"`, no default (E0845). **`lib` prefix (Q4)**: a key that is a link name and starts with `lib` is E0844, with fixes that rename the key and every `@ffi` in one edit or add `libs`. **Duplicates (Q5)**: the link line keeps the last copy of a flag. **Cross target**: a pkg-config entry links static `lib<name>.a` for each name in `libs`; with no `libs` it is E0821, before the link. **Also (U1–U6)**: `system` never runs pkg-config; a failed lookup is E0846 with no `-l<key>` fallback; every entry links, manifest order then `libs` order; examples renamed `sodium`/`curl`/`pq`/`z`; `headers` in the schema. Prefer one entry with `libs` to one entry per library. §9.1, §9.1.2, §8.9, catalog E0820, E0821, E0844–E0846. See [rationale](decisions/native-dependency-link-names.md) | Q1 3-3 then 5-1 after Phase D (Min dissent: key is the `-l` name, no `libs`); cross-target error kept 4-1 among L votes (Sys: link `lib<key>.a`); Q2 5-1 (PLT); Q3 6-0; Q4 5-1 (Min: warning); Q5 6-0; U1–U7 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Constant zero divisor | **Const contexts (Q1)**: in the four const contexts of §2.21, a const expression whose evaluation would panic (integer `/` or `%` by zero, signed `MIN / -1`, overflow) is `ConstEvalPanic` (E0546); the span is the failing subexpression, with a `note:` per `const` reference in the chain. **Expression position (Q2)**: a known-zero divisor outside a const context is not a compile error; it panics at run time. **Warning (V1, V2, V4, N1, N2)**: `DivisionByLiteralZero` (W0704) fires for an integer literal 0 divisor of `/` or `%`, with optional parentheses and unary `-`; not for `const` references or `let` bindings; fires in dead branches (no flow analysis); does not fire inside an `assert_panics` body, a rule for this warning only. **Sibling (Q3, Q3-R)**: `/` and `%` settled here; empty literal ranges out of scope; a future range check must not be a list of APIs built into the typecheck or the stdlib, must come from a rule the API states in its own declaration, and its panel sets the severity. **Side items**: §3.2 says a zero divisor is not overflow (S1); follow-ups on `let` constant expressions (S2) and on lowering a known-zero divisor to an unconditional panic (S3). §2.20, §2.21, §3.2, §3.6 | Q1, Q2, V1, V2, V4, Q3, N1, S1-S3 6-0; N2 3-3 in Phase C → 5-1 `DivisionByLiteralZero` in Phase D (Web dissent); Q3-R r2 4-2 → 6-0, wording 4-2 after runoff (Sys, Min dissent); AI-First 4/5 pass (Consistency fails) |
| Error-severity names in `@allow` / `[lints]` | **Warnings only (Q1)**: `@allow` arguments and `[lints]` keys reach only names under *Warning Names*; the catalog row decides, not effective severity, so a promoted warning stays reachable. **One refusal (Q1, Q2)**: any other live name (error, V0003 included; info; ICE) is `NotAWarningName` E0847, refused not ignored, unsuppressible, every level refused (`"error"` included), each entry reported. **Name (Q3)**: `NotAWarningName`, not `ErrorNotSuppressible`, since info names are in scope. **One rule (Q4)**: the E0842, E0843, lexer and ICE sentences point to it. **Help (Q5)**: remove the entry, then the named error's own help or a catalog repair (V0003 → `@verify(fallback: ...)`, E0501 → widen `@capabilities`); never another `@allow`, never `_ => panic`. **Order (Q6)**: E0842 first; E0837 and E0847 sets do not overlap (audit-gated list holds warnings only). **Named error still fires (Q7)**. **Example (Q8)**: Conventions uses `@allow(UnusedVariable)`. See [rationale](decisions/suppression-reaches-warnings-only.md) | Q1–Q4, Q6, Q7 6-0; Q5 5-1 (Min dissent); Q8 5-1 (AI/ML dissent); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Partial handlers and `@handle_all` | **Derived operations (Q1a, Q1b, Q1c)**: an operation with a body in the effect declaration is derived; if the innermost handler omits it, the body runs at the call site with the same handlers in force, so its calls reach that handler; only an omitted primitive operation goes to the enclosing handler; no outward search for a clause. **Lint (Q2, Q2b, Q2c)**: opt-in `@handle_all` on an effect declaration, covering its sub-effects; stdlib marks `Rand` and `Time` only; fires in all code on a handler expression of a marked effect that omits a primitive operation; a sub-effect handler counts only its own operations. **Name (Q5)**: `PartialHandler` W0545. **Silencer (Q6a)**: write the operation with any body, `@allow` or `[lints]`; no `@trusted` gate. **Fix (Q6b)**: machine-applicable `default.op(args)` stubs with exact signatures. **Runner (Q3, Q4)**: `blink test` installs a seeded `Rand` handler outermost around each test block, seed from suite seed and test name; `prop_check` keeps its own stream; `unseeded_rand_in_test` closed. **Example (Q6c)**: `always_low()` forwards `float` and `bytes`. §4.3, §4.4.1, §4.4.7, §4.7.1, §8.10.4, §11.1, catalog W0545. See [rationale](decisions/partial-handler-lint.md) | Q1a, Q1b, Q2b, Q2c, Q3, Q4, Q5a, Q6a, Q6b 6-0; Q1c, Q2, Q5b, Q6c 5-1 (Min dissent); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Library-panic lint `PanicInLibrary` | **Scope (Q1)**: a package is a library when its entry module `src/<name>.bl` declares no `fn main`; one with `fn main` is an application, also when the `main` is a demo; a file outside a package is never library code. In a library package the lint fires on each reference that resolves to the built-in `panic` outside test blocks, private functions included. **Name**: `PanicInLibrary` W0705. **Level (Q2)**: warn; `@allow` or `[lints]` `"off"`/`"error"`. **Bundle (Q3)**: `@trusted` does not suppress it; no machine-applicable fix; a value use (`let f = panic`) also fires. **Help (Q4)**: one `help:` that names `Result` with the real return type (`Result[(), E]` for `()`, `Result[T, E]` for a type parameter or `Never`, "return `Err(...)`" when it already returns `Result`). **`@requires` (Q5)**: not in the output; `blink explain W0705` names it with "remove the check". **Scope note (Q6)**: a `note:` names the package and its entry file. §2.20, catalog W0705. See [rationale](decisions/library-panic-lint.md) | Q1 5-1 (Min dissent: remove the lint), Q2 6-0, Q3 6-0, Q4 5-1 (Min dissent: always `Result[T, E]`), Q5 6-0 R2 (was 4-2 R1; Sys and PLT changed NOTE→NONE), Q6 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Overflow in literal arithmetic | **Meaning (all)**: "const expression" has meaning only in the four contexts of §2.21; E0546 stays there; `let b: U8 = 300` stays E0318. **Outcome (Q1)**: outside those contexts, overflow in literal-only arithmetic compiles and panics at run time, and the compiler gives the warning `LiteralArithmeticOverflow` (W0708). **Tree (Q2)**: `K ::= N \| L \| "(" K ")" \| "-" K \| K op K` with `op` in `+ - * / %`; a negative literal is matched first, so `-128` is a leaf (E0318) and `-(-128)` at `I8` warns; the grammar is wider than the §3.2 negative-literal rule. **Type (Q3)**: W0708 checks the largest literal-only tree at the type the checker gives each operation; the type of a literal tree in operand position is a separate spec record. **Scope**: a `const` reference or a `let` binding stops the tree; no flow analysis; not inside an `assert_panics` body; one warning for each tree, at the first operation that fails; a literal 0 divisor is W0704 only; `<<` and `>>` are out; not in a const context. **Level**: `@allow(LiteralArithmeticOverflow)`, or `W0708` under `[lints]`; `W0708 = "error"` makes it a build error. §2.20, §2.21, §3.2, §3.6, catalog W0708. See [rationale](decisions/let-constant-overflow.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 5-1 (DevOps dissent); Q3 4-2 in Phase C → 6-0 in Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| `abort` in a handler body | **Construct (Q1)**: Blink has no `abort`; a handler `fn` for an op declared `-> Never` diverges with `panic`, and a test observes it by wrapping the `with` in `assert_panics(matching: ...)`; the undefined `try { }` in §8.10.3 goes too. **Rule (Q5)**: a handler cannot end the `with` body early; production code that needs early exit declares an op whose result type carries the outcome (e.g. `Result[T, E]`) and the caller uses `?`. **`mock_env` (Q2)**: the `exit(code)` panic message contains `env.exit(` + the decimal code + `)`, a stable substring. **Example (Q3)**: the §4.4.3 mock keeps its shared `let mut exit_code` cell, written before the panic, and `assert_eq` after `assert_panics`. **§4.6.3 (Q4)**: the uncaught-panic row drops "abort" for "fatal runtime error" (OOM, stack overflow, foreign code that ends the process; no unwind, no `exit()`/`close()`), with no C function or signal named. **Deferred (Q6, Q7)**: `MockEnv.exit_code()` to its own std.testing ticket; a help line for an unresolved `abort`/`resume` in a handler op body as a diagnostics ticket, no reserved keywords | Q1 6-0, Q2 6-0, Q3 6-0, Q4 5-1 R2 (was 4-1-1 R1; Min dissent: remove the word; term "fatal runtime error" by user), Q5 6-0, Q6 6-0, Q7 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Prelude trait impls for `Never` | **Seven impls (Q1)**: `Never` implements `Eq`, `Ord`, `Hash`, `Clone`, `Debug`, `Display` and `Serialize`; the list is closed. No `Deserialize` (`from_json` is static), `Default`, `From`/`TryFrom` or numeric operators. The §3.6 `Hash` list gains `Never`. **One constraining sentence (Q2)** in §3.2: "Each prelude impl for `Never` holds only methods that take a `Never` argument"; it limits the list and grants nothing. **Diagnostic (Q3)**: a failed bound on `Never` (E0306) gives a `note:` listing the impls (from the checker's table) and a `help:` naming the `match` rewrite when the rewritten type meets the bound, else the failed bound; never "derive it"; no new lint. **No sealing sentence (Q4)**: the orphan rule and *No Impl Overlap* already reject `impl Eq for Never`; the `OrphanImpl` message carries the same note. `"{r}"` on any `Result` still fails (no `Display`); `"{r.debug()}"` works. No rendering of `Never` is specified; codegen lowers each body to a defined trap. §3.2, §3.6 | Q1 4-2 A after Phase C split (A 2, A6 2, B 1, C3 1); plt and min dissent for A6 (no `Hash`); Q2 6-0 L1 after 4-2; Q3 6-0 D1; Q4 6-0 S2 after 3-3; web's re-vote carried from its Phase D reply; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Foreign function declared `-> Never` | **Core (Q1)**: an `@ffi` fn may declare `-> Never`; the claim is part of the signature that `@trusted(audit: K)` records and the pin covers, so `-> Int` → `-> Never` makes the pin stale; no new gate. **The compiler does not assume the claim**: the call has type `Never`, but the claim alone never lets the compiler remove the code after the call; if the foreign function returns, the program panics at the call in every build mode, as if `unreachable()` came after it (the loop-tail release `__builtin_unreachable()` rule is for proved divergence only). **C prototype (Q2)**: the prototype Blink writes never carries `_Noreturn`; a `header:` that declares it noreturn is the C library's claim. **Panic, not fatal (Q3)**: a normal ABI return leaves the process intact; §4.6.3's fatal category is for foreign code that ends the process. **Name (Q4)**: the message starts with `FfiNeverReturned` and names the binding, C symbol, audit key and call site. **Other positions (Q5)**: no `Never`-specific code; `Never` is not FFI-compatible (`Ptr[Never]` E0810, struct field E0812); a general FFI-compatible rule for `@ffi` parameter types, return types and type arguments (it must cover `-> Option[Never]`) goes to a separate ticket, and until then that return-position hole stays open. **W0800 note (Q6)**: states the claim and that a return panics. **Tooling (Q7)**: `blink audit` category and LSP hover left out (tooling chore). **Guidance (Q8)**: declare `-> Never` only when the function returns on no path; `execv` binds `-> I32` and its wrapper returns `Result[Never, E]` as `process.exec` does. §9.1 *`-> Never` — A Foreign Function That Does Not Return*. See [rationale](decisions/ffi-never-return.md) | Q1, Q2, Q3, Q6, Q8 6-0; Q4 5-1 (Min dissent: no stable name); Q5 5-1 (PLT dissent: `FfiNeverPosition`); Q7 5-1 (DevOps dissent: include); user signed off, with the follow-up widened to return types and type arguments; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Shift and bitwise operator precedence | **Partial order (V1, V5, V7)**: §2.19 level 7 holds five integer operator groups (arithmetic, with `* / %` above `+ -`; `<< >>`; `&`; `^`; `\|`) with no order among them; an operand that is a bare expression of a different group is the parse error `MixedOperatorPrecedence` (E1127); the same group chains left (`a << 1 >> 2`, `a \| b \| c`). **Shift vs bitwise (V6)**: a mix is an error, because Go puts `<<` and `&` on one level and `a & b << c` gives a different value in Go than in C. **Comparison (V2, V4)**: every group binds tighter than comparison and equality, so `flags & 0x0F == 0` is `(flags & 0x0F) == 0` and `x << 1 < y` is `(x << 1) < y`; the C grouping of the first never type-checks. **Formatter (V3)**: `blink fmt` prints parentheses around a bitwise or shift operand of a comparison or equality. **Diagnostics (V8)**: the `help:` line shows both groupings; the language server offers both rewrites, neither machine-applicable; W0702 `BitwisePrecedence` retired, code never reused; `&`/`\|`/`^` on `Bool` is a type error whose help names `&&`/`\|\|`. **Unary (V9)**: `~` joins `-` and `!`. §2.19 *Integer Operator Groups*, §3.2. See [rationale](decisions/shift-bitwise-precedence.md) | V1, V5, V7, V9 6-0; V2 6-0 R2 (was 3-2-1: 2B 3, 2C 2, 2A 1); V6 6-0 R2 (was 4-2; Web and Min moved); V3 5-1 (Sys dissent: drop the parens); V4 5-1 (PLT dissent: parse error); V8 5-1 (Min dissent, lapsed with 2A); user signed off; AI-First 5/5 pass |

Amends the E0601 field-path Q3b. **`mut` fix (Q5)**: machine-applicable for a local plain-name `let` only (pattern binders: amended by [`mut` in patterns](decisions/mut-in-patterns.md)); module-level `let` help only (`State` in far rows); imported `pub let` names E1013. **Errors at once (Q6)**; ParamAssign keeps warn-first. **Q7**: capture label, `blink fix` applies identical edits once (§8.6), not-atomic note, tests, E0611 onward. | Enum Ord: fieldless implicit, data enums derive | **Rule (Q1)**: a fieldless enum is `Ord` by declaration order with no derive, as the real instance (`.cmp`, `T: Ord`, tuples, `@derive(Ord)` fields); a written `impl Ord` on it is a compile error; a redundant `@derive(Ord)` is legal, no warning; an enum with at least one payload is `Ord` only through `@derive(Ord)` or a written `impl Ord`; `<` on a data enum with no `Ord` is a normal diagnostic, never an ICE. **Str-backed (Q2)**: declaration order, not backing-string order. **Guarantee (Q3)**: for a fieldless `E`, `a < b` exactly when `a.to_int() < b.to_int()` (also `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `cmp`), a compiler guarantee, not a user law. **Diagnostic (Q4)**: one code for a written `impl Eq` or `impl Ord` on a fieldless enum; names the trait, gives the reason, second label on the `type` line, help gives the fix. **Framing (Q5)**: the declaration gives `Eq` and `Ord` as an implicit derive, only these two; explicit derive is the same instance; the first payload removes both. **Generic (Q6)**: `type Tag[T] { A  B }` is `Eq` and `Ord` with no bound on `T`. **Custom order (Q7)**: inline help names a named key method with `sort_by`; a struct wrapper with its own `impl Ord` second, for a `T: Ord` bound. **Placement (Q8)**: §3.6 next to the Eq rule; §3.4 *Tag Operations* gets a cross-reference, not a fourth tag operation. **Formatter (Q9)**: `blink fmt` never reorders variants. §3.6 *Container Equality* (*Enum Ord*), §3.4 *Tag Operations*, §8.8. | Field checks in struct and variant literals (unknown, duplicate, missing; `..` on a variant) | **Core (Q1)**: the labels of a literal form a set; an unknown label is `NoSuchField` (E0525, widened from field access); a label given twice is `DuplicateField` (E0316) at the second label; a field with no default and no label, and no `..source`, is `MissingField` (E0315), one per literal, listing each field and type. Applies to struct literals, qualified and bare variant brace literals, copy-update and the §2.15 shorthand; checked by `blink check`. **Convergence**: a missing field that is the unique suggestion of an unknown label leaves the `MissingField` list. **No machine fix (Q2)** for `MissingField` (no `todo()`) or `DuplicateField`; the `NoSuchField` rename is machine-applicable with one suggestion. **Paren payload (Q3)**: all labelled gives the same three names in place of the arity error; a positional payload keeps the arity `TypeError` plus a note listing the fields. **`..source` on a variant (Q4)**: `VariantSpread` (E0317). **Variant field defaults (Q5)**: none; "(or defaulted)" struck from §3.4, deferred to its own spec question. **§3.4 (Q6)**: "Variant construction does not accept `..source`; copy-update (§2.16) applies to struct literals only. Every variant field must be supplied." **Declaration edits in help (Q7)**: only for a type declared in the package being compiled. **Order (Q8, Q11c)**: Duplicate, then NoSuchField, then Missing; source order inside each kind. **Patterns (Q9)**: not covered; the row names literals only. **Nearest-name rule (Q10)**: one *Conventions* rule in ERROR_CATALOG. **Help (Q11a, Q11b)**: no suggestion lists the declared fields; a shorthand diagnostic names the inferred type and the parameter. §2.14 *Checking a Literal's Fields*, §2.15, §2.16, §3.4 *Variant Construction*. See [rationale](decisions/literal-field-checks.md) | 6-0 on all questions (Q1–Q10, Q11a–c); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| W0611 default severity | **Warning by default (Q1)**: W0611 `ScopedValueEnteredTwice` stays a warning; `[lints] W0611 = "error"` raises it per project, `@allow(ScopedValueEnteredTwice)` suppresses it per function. The corpus data from the change that adds its emit site starts an edition review (§8.16.1). **Conditions in decisions/ only (Q1b)**: no sections/ text about a later raise; the review needs an emit site, the per-path rule, a corpus and lib/std hit count with false positives defined, and the `TcpSocket` second close fixed. **Warning name kept (Q1c)**: a later raise changes only the default level, so `@allow` and `[lints]` still reach it. **Per-path rule (Q2a, Q2b, Q2c)**: W0611 counts the `with` items one execution path takes the value into; `if`/`else` branches and `match` arms count once; a `with` in a loop body counts as two unless every path after it leaves the loop; a copy is the same value. **Text (Q3)**: the "not a proof" sentence adds "W0611 is not a safety check: a type that wraps a foreign handle should make a second `exit()` safe (§4.7.2)". **Note (Q4)**: the sample adds the `[lints] W0611 = "error"` note, as W0610's does. **Q5**: a raise puts the default level in a catalog column. **Q6**: precedence is `@allow` > `[lints]` > catalog default; "deny" stays separate. **Q7**: the `TcpSocket` second-close bug blocks the next stdlib release. **Q8**: a ticket records the lack of a dependency lint cap. §5.5, ERROR_CATALOG.md. See [rationale](decisions/w0611-default-severity.md) | Q1 6-0 R2 after 4-2 R1 (Web, AI/ML for error by default); Q1b 3-3 R2 → user broke tie for no spec text (Sys, Web, DevOps dissent); Q1c 6-0; Q2a 6-0; Q2b 5-1 (Min dissent: explain-only); Q2c 6-0; Q3 4-2 R2 after 4-1-1 R1 (Web, DevOps for a separate sentence); Q4 6-0; Q5 5-0 (Min n/a); Q6 6-0; Q7 6-0; Q8 5-1 (Min dissent); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| JSON encoding of NaN and infinity | **`null` (Q1)**: `json.stringify`, `json.pretty` and `json.encode` write a `JsonValue.Float` holding NaN, `inf` or `-inf` as `null` and stay infallible; `json.parse` rejects `NaN`/`Infinity`/`-Infinity` in any ASCII case; a derived `from_json` for a `Float` field rejects `null`; `Option[Float]` reads `null` as `None`. **No strict check now (Q2)**: if one is added later, its shape is `json.check(value: JsonValue) -> Result[(), JsonError]`. **Parse message (Q3)**: names the rejected token; wording not fixed. **Decode message (Q4)**: says the value was `null` and the field expects `Float`; the `Option[Float]` hint is non-normative impl text. **Laws (Q5)**: valid output, and a round trip with each non-finite Float replaced by `Null`; an `Option[Float]` holding `Some` of a non-finite Float decodes as `None`. **`JsonValue` equality (Q5b)**: structural `Eq`; `Object` compares as a list (order and duplicate keys count); not `Hash`. §3.6, §3.6.2. See [rationale](decisions/json-nan-infinity.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0; Q3 6-0 R2 after 3-2-1 R1; Q4 3-3 R2 after 4-1-1 R1 → user broke tie for content rule (Web, DevOps, AI/ML for a SHOULD hint); Q5 5-1 (Min: one law); Q5b 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Float bits | **API (Q1)**: built-in `to_bits()` / static `from_bits()` on `Float` (`U64`) and `F32` (`U32`), no import; `from_bits` is total; no hex float text form, no NaN canonicalization. §3c.3 now covers only `to_X`/`to_X_checked` where `X` names a type, so `to_bits` and `Char.to_digit` are not conversions. **Sign (Q2)**: built-in `is_sign_negative()` (the sign bit, NaN included) replaces `1.0 / x < 0.0`; no `copysign`, `signum` or `is_sign_positive`. **Help (Q3a)**: a bitwise operator on a `Float` names `to_bits()`. **Follow-ups**: the `-0.0` literal warning, before the next release (Q3b); const `from_bits` with `const fn` (Q4); `U64` literals at or above `0x8000000000000000` (Q7). **Laws (Q5)**: round trip with signaling NaNs; equal bits imply `==`, not the converse; arithmetic does not fix a NaN payload; the sign law; copies, storage and `@ffi` keep every bit. **Placement (Q6)**: a `####` *Float Bits* heading after *Float Total Ordering*, scope fixed to these methods and laws. §3.6, §3c.3, llms-full.md. See [rationale](decisions/float-bits.md) | Q1, Q2, Q3a, Q4, Q7 6-0; Q3b 4-2 (DevOps, AI/ML dissent: in this change), soft consensus; Q5 L1–L5 6-0, L6 excluded 5-1 (AI/ML dissent); Q6 4-2 R2 after 3-3 R1 (Sys, Min dissent: bold run-in); user signed off; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Negative zero comparison | **Warning**: `NegativeZeroComparison` (W0706) fires when an operand of a `Float`/`F32` comparison is a written negative zero (one unary `-` over a zero float literal, parentheses allowed; `-(-0.0)` does not match), read as written: no `const` or `let` following (Q4), no folding, no flow analysis. **Relations (Q1, Q2, Q6)**: calls that resolve to the concrete `Float`/`F32` impl of `Eq` (`==`, `!=`, `eq`, `ne`), `Ord` (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `cmp`), `ieee_eq`, and `assert_eq`/`assert_ne`; checked in typecheck on the resolved callee, so no diagnostics from mono; `min`, `max` and `contains` out. **Both literal (Q3)**: fires; the *Float Total Ordering* example keeps its literal lines marked `// warning[NegativeZeroComparison]` (Q3b). Fires in dead branches, test blocks and `assert_panics` bodies. **Patterns (Q5)**: a future pattern form that can write `-0.0` must come under W0706 or be an error. **Fix (Q7)**: no machine-applicable fix for any form. **Diagnostic (Q8)**: span on the operand; help names `is_sign_negative()`, puts in a simple-path operand or binds a `let` first and never repeats a call; a `note:` that `0.0` means the same; `blink explain` uses `to_bits()`. `@allow(NegativeZeroComparison)`; `W0706` under `[lints]`. §3.6. See [rationale](decisions/negative-zero-comparison.md) | Q1, Q2, Q4, Q6, Q8 6-0; Q3 6-0 R2 after 4-2 R1 (DevOps, AI/ML for Exempt); Q3b 6-0; Q5 5-1 (Min dissent: no spec text); Q7 5-1 (PLT dissent: ordering-only fix); user signed off; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Integer literal range | **Value (Q1)**: an integer literal is the exact integer its digits spell, in every base, never a bit pattern; it must fit its type, else `IntLiteralOutOfRange` (E0318) at the literal, one code for every integer type, no `@allow`; with no expected type the message names the default `Int` and a help offers `U64` when the value fits; above the `U64` maximum, no help. So `let m: U64 = 9223372036854775808` is valid and `let x = 9223372036854775808` is E0318. **Negative literals (Q2, Q3)**: one unary `-` on an integer literal, parentheses allowed (`-(128)`), is one literal; signed types check the negative value (`-9223372036854775808` is the `Int` minimum); under an unsigned type every negative literal is E0318, `-0` included. **Operators (Q4)**: a literal operand of `+ - * / % & \| ^ == != < <= > >=` takes the other operand's integer type; an expected `T` reaches both operands of `+ - * / % & \| ^`; shift amounts are `U32`; §3.2 is the one statement of these positions. **Timing (Q5)**: the type is fixed at the literal; no deferred literal type. **Patterns (Q6)** and **const contexts (Q7, not E0546)** use E0318. §3.2 *Literal syntax*, §3.3, ERROR_CATALOG. See [rationale](decisions/integer-literal-range.md) | Q1–Q7 6-0; unanimous fast path; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Module-level initializers | **Static only (Q1)**: the initializer of a module-level `let` / `let mut` must be a static expression: a const expression, list or tuple literal, struct literal, enum variant (parts static), an empty-collection constructor, or a function literal (body not run at init; its effects go in the literal's type). No calls, `if`/`match`/block/`with`, `return` or `?`; no user code runs before `main`, so the order is not defined and cannot change a value, and no cycle can occur. Rejected: any expression with an empty effect row (calls allowed, dependency order, cycle check through fn bodies). **No reads of module `let` (Q2)**: `const` refs only. **Constructors (Q3)**: `[]`, `Map()`, `Set()`, `Map.new[K, V]()`, `Set.new[T]()`, `List.new()`, `List.new[T]()`; no `List.with_capacity(n)`. **Interpolation (Q4)**: not static. **Function literals (Q5)**: no capture rule. **Diagnostic (Q6)**: one code `NonInitExpr` (E1129), label names the first bad part, help gives the compute-in-`main` / fill-a-`let mut` fix and its `State` cost; not E1101 or E0508/E0509. **Rollout (Q7)**: a warning with the final code and message first, then an error at a fixed release. Closes *`return` / `?` in a top-level `let`*. §2.12.1, §2.21, ERROR_CATALOG.md. See [rationale](decisions/module-level-initializers.md) | Q1, Q2, Q4, Q5, Q6 6-0; Q3 5-1 (Min dissent: `[]`, `Map()`, `Set()` only); Q7 5-1 (Min dissent: error now); user signed off; AI-First 4/5 pass (token efficiency fails: a computed table needs the memo recipe) |

| Keyword marker re-audit (`*` replaces `--`) | **Marker (Q1)**: `fn transfer(amount: Int, *, from: Account, to: Account)`; every §2.13 rule and the five codes (E0510, E0529, E0511, E0527, E0528) stay. **Rejected rows (Q2)**: "Function parameter defaults" becomes "Defaults on positional parameters (E0531)"; "Mixed positional + keyword" becomes "Caller-chosen labels"; "Optional keyword args (caller's choice)" stays. **Migration (Q3)**: `--` in a param list is an error at once, with a machine-applicable edit to `*,`; the parser recovers at the marker, a test proves `blink fix` applies it, and the edit stays as long as `--` stays reserved. **`--` token (Q4)**: stays a reserved token with no meaning; prefix `--x` and infix `a--b` get help only; the lexical rule is in §2.19, the message wording is not. **E0529 (Q5)**: help only, no machine-applicable fix; the help names the params and does not assign an order. **Trait/impl conformance (Q6)**: a separate ruling. **Defaults (Q7)**: one rule in §2.13: full-arity fn type; a direct call fills const defaults after all written args evaluate; a fn value takes every argument; bare-type-param defaults stay open. **Bug-class text (Q8)**: drop the uncited "3-8%" figure; state that the guard covers direct calls and method calls on a concrete receiver, not fn values, and not trait-bound calls until the conformance ruling. **Grammar (Q9)**: a newline after the marker is allowed; at most one marker; a marker with no keyword param after it is an error. | Map/Set insertion order re-audit | **Order (Q1)**: `Map` and `Set` iterate in insertion order; `insert` on a present key replaces the value and keeps the position; `remove` keeps the relative order of the rest; a re-insert after `remove` appends; `keys()`, `values()`, `entries()` and `for` share one order. The hash seed stays random per process for bucketing only (hash flooding) and has no effect on output; the layout goes in docs/build/, not the spec. **W1401 (Q2)**: `MapOrderAssumption` withdrawn; the code is not reused. **Flags (Q3)**: `--deterministic` and `BLINK_MAP_SEED` leave the spec; `--deterministic` stays one release as a no-op that prints a note, then goes; `BLINK_MAP_SEED` is an internal knob. **Reuse (Q4)**: a compiler may reuse a pure call's result only for arguments identical in representation; `==` does not license reuse; the Map-specific purity exclusion goes. **Derived (Q5)**: every derived `Map`/`Set` is built by inserts: `clone` keeps order; `a.union(b)` is `a`'s order, then `b`'s new elements; `collect()` keeps the first position and the last value of a duplicate key. **Debug (Q6)**: the `==`-maps-can-iterate-differently fact is stated once in §3.6; `debug()` text points to it. **Serializers (Q7)**: std serializers encode in iteration order; std deserializers add entries in document order, and the first entry of a duplicate key gives position and value. | Q1 6-0, Q2 6-0, Q3 6-0, Q4 5-1 (devops dissent: keep the exclusion with its reason fixed), Q5 6-0, Q6 6-0 R2 after 4-2 R1, Q7 6-0 R2 after 5-1 R1 on a misworded option |
| Annotation list (one normative table) | **One table (V0)**: §11.1 is the one normative list of annotations, with targets, repeats, meaning and status; every other list points to it; the set is closed and a row change needs a panel vote. New rows: `@module`, `@pure`, `@tags`, `@ffi.struct`, `@ffi.opaque`, `@skip`, `@deprecate_override`. **Unsupported rows (Q1)**: a row works only when its checker exists (the compiler, or the named tool); otherwise `AnnotationNotSupported`. **Tool rows (Q2)**: `@perf`, `@alt`, `@src` stay, status tool-checked. **`@modifies` (Q3)**: removed; a use is `UnknownAnnotation` with a hint that names the `State` effect. **`@expect_panic` (Q4)**: removed; tests use `assert_panics`. **`@skip` (Q5)**: stays. **Derive (Q6)**: seven traits (Eq, Ord, Hash, Clone, Debug, Serialize, Deserialize); Display is not derivable (E1402); derive laws are normative. **Eq (Q6-Eq)**: `@derive(Ord)` or `@derive(Hash)` auto-derives `Eq`, but a hand-written `Eq` with a derived `Hash` or `Ord` is `DeriveOverHandWrittenEq`. **`@deprecate_override` (Q7)**: its own row. **Details (Q8)**: `@verify` fallback `runtime \| error \| trust`; `@trusted` needs `audit:`; structured `@deprecated`; `@src` on fn, type, module; `@allow` on fn, type. **Rules (Q9)**: a repeat is `DuplicateAnnotation` unless the row allows it; status is `checked \| tool-checked`. | Handler delegation row | The handler type carries its clause row: `Handler[E ! R]` (row inside the brackets); `Handler[E]` means R is empty. R` into the declared type. A forward reaches the handler in force for E at the `with` that installed the handler (install). `with a, b` = `with a { with b }`; the "leftmost wins" text goes. `Handler[E ! _]` ships in parameter types. Amends Q7 (patterns) and the pattern-binder part of W1011 for refutable positions; the bare payload pattern on a struct, tuple or type parameter moves from E0547 to E0308. Rejected: T-enum, T-near, T-warn, a redundant-qualifier lint. §3.4, §3.5, §10.6, §2.20, ERROR_CATALOG. The O(n²) and "ambiguity" reasons are removed. **Law (D3)**: §3.6 states commutativity only (`a + b == b + a`, or both panic; Float `==` is the total order, so every NaN is equal); no associativity law. **Diagnostic (Q2)**: `+` and `+=` on `Str` are E0521 `StrPlusUnsupported`, one diagnostic per `+` chain; the fix is one interpolation, machine-applicable only when every operand is `Str`, otherwise a suggestion with the exact text. | Field faults in struct and variant brace patterns | **Checks**: the labels of a struct pattern and of a variant brace pattern (bare and qualified) are checked at every depth and in every pattern position; an unknown label is `NoSuchField` (E0525, widened), a label given twice is `DuplicateField` (E0316, widened), fields left out with no `..` are `PatternMissingRest` (E0319, Q1). **Order**: after the type-name checks, duplicate, then unknown, then missing rest. **Fixes**: for an unknown punned label the machine fix writes `y: yy`; no "or rename the binder" line (Q2); no "or add the field to `T`" line. A field default does not count as naming the field. **After the error**: E0525 and E0316 make a bad arm; `PatternMissingRest` alone does not (the arm is checked as if `..` were there); only binders under an unknown label have the poison type. **Grammar**: gains the qualified variant brace form. §3.5 *Struct patterns*, *After the error*, *An arm with a bad pattern covers nothing*. | Contract that always fails (V0002) | **Rule (Q1)**: V0002 is warning `ContractAlwaysFails` (Q2), not a compile error. It fires when a `@requires`, or a `@where` refinement as an implied `@requires` (Q3), is false on every run that reaches the call; the runtime check stays. With no solver, it fires when every argument the predicate reads is a literal at the call site and the folded predicate is `false`. **Literal (Q6)**: the W0708 literal tree plus list, string and range literals whose parts are literal trees; the predicate folds in the const evaluator behind E0546; a `let` or `const` reference stops the tree. **Fold failure (Q9)**: a fold that panics, a predicate that cannot be evaluated, or the evaluator limit gives no V0002; the outcome is Unknown. **Shape**: the shape of W0704 (no `let`/`const` reference, fires in dead branches, silent inside `assert_panics`, `@allow`, `[lints]`); only `[lints]` changes its level. **`@verify` (Q4)**: V0002 fires under every value, `"trust"` included; a counterexample on a dynamic input is Unknown, so `"error"` gives V0003. **Counterexample (Q5)**: shown in a note on V0004, in the `blink verify` report and in V0003 under `"error"`. **Solver (Q7)**: a solver may add a V0002 hit only where it proves that some run reaches the call and that the predicate is false on every run that reaches it; one counterexample never counts; literal hits fire in dead branches. **Out (Q8, Q10)**: `@ensures` is not part of V0002; no `rand` API change, and `Range[T]` bound accessors are a separate decision. **Heading (Q11)**: §3b.4 "Always Fails (Warning)"; the §3b.1 and §3b.2 examples change. §3b.1, §3b.2, §3b.4.
