| CPU intrinsics and target features | No CPU surface in Blink; CPU-specific code is C behind @ffi; baseline = toolchain default CPU per triple, never raised; no float-changing flags; @trusted claim covers the CPU; std fast paths bit-identical to portable. §9.1 *CPU Baseline and Accelerated Code*. See [rationale](decisions/cpu-intrinsics.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0; Q3 (runtime SIGILL message as chore) 4-1-1 in Phase C → 6-0 in Phase D; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Return with a value in a function that returns `()` | **Rule (Q1)**: `return e` checks `e` against the return type of the nearest enclosing function or closure for every return type, `()` included; a mismatch is `TypeError` (E0300), with no new code. `return`, `return ()`, `return e` with `e: ()` and `return e` with `e: Never` stay legal in a `()` function. **Tail**: the tail drop is unchanged; it applies to the result of the last statement, and the operand of `return` is never dropped. **Closures**: with an expected function type the return type comes from that type; with none it is the one type on which every `return e` and a tail that can complete agree, and `()` when there is none; a `Never` operand adds no constraint, a tail that can complete with `()` after a valued `return` is `MissingReturn` (E0311), and exits that disagree are E0300. **Diagnostic**: the help names two fixes in order, add `-> T`, then drop the value (a call stays as its own statement); the error points to the omitted `-> R` or the expected parameter type. **Wording (Q2)**: "(equivalently `-> Void`)" is deleted from *Divergence and Body Completeness*. §3.3. See [rationale](decisions/unit-return-value.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0 |
| Jumps as the right operand of `??` | **Jump (Q3)**: `return`, `return e`, `break` and `continue` are "jumps": type `Never`, not expressions, standing in four positions only (statement, block tail, braceless arm body, right operand of `??`). **Rule (Q1)**: the right operand of `??` is an expression or a jump, so `o ?? return e` is legal. **Parentheses (Q2)**: `o ?? (return 0)` is `JumpNotAllowedHere` (E1133) and the fix drops the parentheses. **Carried**: the operand of `return` is greedy (`o ?? return a ?? b` is `o ?? return (a ?? b)`); E1133 replaces the keyword-as-identifier error for a jump; `blink fmt` writes `?? { return e }` as `?? return e` and keeps a one-jump arm on one line. §2.11 *Jumps*, §3.3. See [rationale](decisions/return-in-expression.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 5-1 (Web dissent); Q3 6-0 |
| Keyword parameters in trait impls | **The trait governs (Q1-Q3)**: the trait method's declaration governs every call path (`b.k(1)`, `t.k(1)` with `T: Kw`, `Kw.k(b, 1)`); fn types stay label-free. **Q2**: an impl repeats the `*` marker position and the keyword names in the trait's order; positional names stay free. **Q1**: an impl writes no default on a trait method's keyword parameter and inherits the trait's default on every path, even a default of the same value (as E0912 for `@requires`). **Q3**: `E0914 TraitContractKeywordMismatch` (marker, names, order; its `help:` prints the trait's parameter list with no defaults) and `E0915 TraitImplWritesDefault` (a default written in the impl); E0914 comes first when both fire. **Fixes**: marker and order machine-applicable; a rename only when one-to-one by position and free in the body; deleting a const-equal default machine-applicable; a differing or impl-only default help only. §3.6 *Keyword Parameters in Impls*; the §2.13 *Limit of the guard* trait-bound clause is gone. See [rationale](decisions/trait-impl-keyword-params.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0; Q3 3-3 in Phase C → 5-1 in Phase D (aiml dissent) |
| Handler clause diagnostics | **Rule**: a handler clause names one operation of the handled effect and matches its header exactly, for user effects and every built-in effect: arity, parameter types and result type (exact equality after alias expansion), the `*,` marker and the keyword names (positional names are free), and no default. A missing clause stays legal. **Codes**: `HandlerClauseMismatch` (E0548), `UnknownHandlerOp` (E0549), `DuplicateHandlerClause` (E0550, second and later clauses only; only the first clause is checked for E0548) (Q1 S3, Q2 N2). **Fixes**: delete a default and fix `*,` and keyword order are machine-applicable; add a missing parameter is machine-applicable when its name is not free in the body and its place is clear (Q4 M1); a header rewrite that changes a type is a suggestion. §4.7 *Defining a handler*, §4.4.8. See [rationale](decisions/handler-clause-diagnostics.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 2-2-2 in Phase C → 5-1 in Phase D; Q3 6-0; Q4 5-1; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Variant hint positions and global-unique reach | A closed list of positions gives a bare variant its expected type (branches and block tails inherit it; a generic parameter gives none); the global-unique step searches the module, the package's pub enums, the prelude, and enums imported by name; private enums of other modules never count. §3.4 *Resolution order*, *Expected type at a site*. See [rationale](decisions/variant-hint-positions.md) Amended by *Qualified variants in expressions*. | 6-0, 6-0 |
| Impl-chosen effect rows for cleanup traits | `Closeable.close`, `Scoped.enter` and `Scoped.exit` declare `! _`: each impl names its own row, and E0904 does not bound it; a concrete call is charged that row, a call through `T` is charged `_` and the enclosing function needs `_` (E0500), and `_` resolves from the type arguments' impl rows at a generic call, from signatures only. A `_` in a function or impl-method row needs a source (a `! _` parameter, or a type parameter whose bound has a `_` method); with none it is `UnsourcedEffectWildcard` (E0551). Effects beside an impl-chosen `_` limit no impl | Q1 F1 3-2-1 after Phase D (plt, aiml dissent for reject; web for a lint); Q2 6-0 |
| Ord, Clone and JSON for Option and Result | **Ord (Q1, Q2)**: `Option[T]: Ord` when `T: Ord`; `Result[T, E]: Ord` when `T: Ord` and `E: Ord`; variant first, then payload; `None < Some(_)`, `Ok(_) < Err(_)`; the `Option` declaration lists `None` first and §3.6 states both orders in words. **Clone (Q3)**: `Option[T]: Clone` when `T: Clone`; `Result[T, E]: Clone` when `T: Clone` and `E: Clone`; the payload's `clone` runs, so `x.clone()` gives the same value as `let y = x`. **Framing (Q4)**: one rule sentence says `Option` and `Result` have the instances `@derive` gives a user sum type with the same variants, for `Eq`, `Ord`, `Clone` and `Debug`; JSON has its own rows. **Option decode (Q5)**: `Option[T]: Deserialize` when `T: Deserialize`; `null` is `None`; any other value is `Some` of the decoded `T`. **`Option[Option[T]]` (Q6)**: the instance stays parametric; the round-trip law does not hold for `Some(v)` where `v.to_json()` is `null`; `@derive(Serialize)` or `@derive(Deserialize)` on a field declared `T??`, after alias expansion and before generic substitution, is E1401. **`Result` JSON (Q7)**: `Result[T, E]` is `Serialize` and `Deserialize` when `T` and `E` are; `Ok(v)` is `{"Ok": v}` and `Err(e)` is `{"Err": e}`; decode needs an object with exactly one entry, key `"Ok"` or `"Err"`, and any other input is a `JsonError` that names both keys, says case matters and gives the path. §3.6, §3.6.1, §3.6.2, §7.3. See [rationale](decisions/option-result-ord-clone-json.md) | Q1, Q3, Q4, Q5 6-0; Q2 5-1 (Min dissent: D2); Q6 6-0 after 4-1-1; Q7 4-2 R1 in Phase D after 3-3 (Web and AI/ML dissent: R2s) → **Owner amendment**: R2s (owner sided with Web and AI/ML); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Newtype: one-field struct | The newtype is a one-field struct (`type Errno { code: Int }`); a type alias is not a newtype; no tuple struct and no `newtype` keyword; name the field for what it holds, with `value` as the fallback; JSON follows struct rules (`{"code":2}`); a variant with the name of its own enum is E0518 at the variant. §3.4 *Newtypes*, *Name collisions*. See [rationale](decisions/newtype.md) | Q1 S 6-0; Q2 G 5-1 (Minimalism dissent); Q3 file an `@json(transparent)` ticket 6-0 after 4-2; self-named variant error 6-0, E0518 6-0 |
| A type named like a variant | **Legal (Q1, this text: L, owner amendment)**: an enum, alias, trait or effect named like a variant is not a collision at its declaration; E0518 stays on the brace form (struct) only. A bare variant `X` or `X(...)` is `VariantNamedLikeType` (E0554) when a type named `X` is in scope, in any expression (a hint does not help), builtin and prelude types included; a pattern still finds the variant from the matched value's type; write `Enum.X`. The own-enum exemption is removed: a variant with its own enum's name is E0518 (see the newtype decision). **Dotted head**: at `Name.x` a value comes first, then a name in the type namespace (type path, no fallback on the member set), then a bare variant; `Color.Red.x()` reaches the variant. **Help**: a failed type path with a bare variant of that name in reach names the qualified variant. **W1011 (Q3)**: at the type declaration, once for each hidden variant of a different enum; a dependency variant counts only when an import entry names its enum. §3.4, §10.6. See [rationale](decisions/type-variant-shadow.md) Amended by *Qualified variants in expressions*. | Q1 3-3 after Phase D -> owner tiebreak L, amended; Q2 V1-all 5-1 (counts only under E); Q3 W2 6-0 after Phase D |
| Mutation lints and the dynamic-binding form | **Binding pair (N)**: a save is `let x = G` or `let x = G.clone()` of a module `let mut` `G`; a restore is `G = x`; a call is in a binding of `G` when an assignment to `G` other than the restore comes after the save and before that call. W0550 and W0551 do not check a call for a pair that is in a binding at that call, and a binding pair does not make a speculative context. Judged per call, also in a function with both kinds of pair. **Any other assignment counts (Q1)**: a callee that also writes `G` does not fire; seek-and-try (`pos = start`, then `parse_item()`) and step-then-try (`pos = pos + 1`, then `try_x()`) read as bindings, a known hole stated in the spec. **Fire text (Q2)**: a `note:` that names the pair and its class, plus one `help:` line that names the binding shape; no `@allow` line. **Exit paths**: neither lint looks at them, so a `?` or `return` between the assignment and the restore gives no warning, a known limit stated in the spec; the follow-up ticket for it holds the close gate (Q3). **Construct (Q3)**: no separate spec ticket for a scoped-binding construct; the exit-path ticket cannot close until its record compares an exit-path warning with the construct. §4.16.7. See [rationale](decisions/mutation-lint-binding-pair.md) | N agreed 6-0; Q1 A 5-1 (PLT dissent: S); Q2 H1 5-1 (Web dissent: H2); Q3 C2 + close gate 6-0 after 3-3 |
| Mutation lints: return write set | W0550/W0551 read the callee's return write set: writes on paths that reach a normal return; a path ends only at a §3.3 `Never` expression, no inferred `-> Never`; no Env.Exit handler exception; help names write site + call chain | Q1, Q2, Q3 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Qualified variants in expressions | **Rule (Q1, Q4)**: in an expression, a user variant carries its enum in all three forms (`Color.Green`, `Shape.Circle(1.0)`, `QueryError.NotFound { ... }`); patterns do not change. **Bare list (Q2)**: exactly `Some`, `None`, `Ok` and `Err`, the `Option` and `Result` variants; the list is closed; `Ordering`, `JsonValue` and every other enum are qualified; `Option.Some(1)` stays legal. **Types (Q3)**: a type named one of the four is E1016, also at an import entry; the help says rename or alias. **Dotted heads (Q5)**: step 3 covers only the four (`None.is_none()`); `Red.x` is always the type or value `Red`. **Diagnostic (Q6)**: E0554 now means `UnqualifiedVariant` ("variant `Green` needs its enum in an expression", help `Color.Green`), machine-applicable when one enum has the variant. **Codes (Q7)**: E1016 and W1011 narrowed to the four names; E0519 retired; E0518 unchanged (follow-up). An expected type only types a literal and infers `T` for the four; the global-unique search finds help candidates only. **Implementation order (Q8, not spec text)**: a warning with a machine fix, then migrate the tree, then the error. §2.12.1, §3.4, §3.5, §10.6, ERROR_CATALOG. See [rationale](decisions/qualified-variants.md) Amended by *Variant and static method name case*. | 6-0 (Q1-Q7); Q8 impl order 5-1, Minimalism dissent |
| Variant and static method name case | **Variants (Q1)**: a variant name must start with an uppercase letter (Unicode `Lu`/`Lt`); `x`, `_x`, `_X` and a caseless first letter are `LowercaseVariant` (E0555, "variant name must start with an uppercase letter") at the declaration. The type-directed branch of the pattern rule goes away: in a refutable pattern a bare name that does not start with an uppercase letter is a binding with no payload, and E0547 (enum) or E0308 (not an enum) with one. `K.lower` never names a variant. **Static methods (Q2)**: the name after `T.` in `fn T.name` must not start with `Lu`/`Lt` (`UppercaseTypeScopedFn`, E0556; the message says an uppercase name after `T.` is a variant and gives the lowercase name); so `K.Upper` never names a function, and on an enum a miss is E0547. **Help (Q3)**: only the nearest-name convention (ERROR_CATALOG *Conventions*), with the variants of `K` added to its candidates; no new help text. **Fix (Q4)**: machine-applicable only when the first character maps to exactly one `Lu`/`Lt` character and `K` has no member with the new name; it renames the declaration and every use (every module of the package for a `pub` type); one mistake, one diagnostic. Writer's reading, not voted: type-scoped constants (`MsgFlags.NONE`) stay reachable under either case; the Q2 rule also covers an associated function that a trait declares. Rejected: a warning lint (B), an ICE-only fix (C); for Q2, variants-only with an unreachable method (A) and variants-then-methods (B); for Q3, a case-insensitive variant help line (A). Amends Q4 of *Variant spelling and the uppercase pattern rule* and the `K.name` lookup of *Qualified variants in expressions*. §2, §2.12.3, §3.4, §3.5, §3c.4, ERROR_CATALOG. See [rationale](decisions/variant-name-case.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0 R2 after 4-2; Q3 6-0 R2 after 4-2; Q4 6-0 |
| Payload variants as fn values | **Rule (Q1)**: a variant with a positional payload, named with no argument list, is a fn value of type `fn(P1, ..., Pn) -> E[binders]` (n parameters, not a tuple; empty effect row); §3.4 *A generic function named as a value* solves the binders, and when nothing fixes them it is E0301 at the reference (`let f = Some`; `xs.map(Ok)` with `E` unfixed). Labels of a labelled positional payload drop. A variant with named fields is not a fn value: E0300, "variant `Shape.Circle` has named fields and is not a function value", closure help. A no-payload variant stays a value of the enum, never `fn() -> E`. As a value, `Ok` is only the one-argument form. A bare user variant is still E0554 first. **`Ok()` (Q2)**: not part of this ruling. **Mismatch help (Q3, Q4)**: bare `Ok` against `fn() -> Result[(), E]` is E0300 with help `fn() { Ok(()) }`; `Color.Red` against `fn() -> Color` is E0300 with help `fn() { Color.Red }`. **E0300 (Q5)**: narrowed, not deleted; it stays the code for an arity mismatch against an expected fn type; only the "not a function value" message goes for positional variants. Static methods as values: out of scope. §3.4, ERROR_CATALOG. See [rationale](decisions/variant-fn-value.md) | Q1-Q5 6-0; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Defaults on generic parameters | A keyword default and a struct field default are checked once, at the declaration, against the declared type with each type parameter abstract, so a default must fit every instance (`x: T = 3` is an error; `x: Option[T] = None` and `xs: List[T] = []` fit). An omitted argument adds no constraint to inference: `T` comes from the written arguments, the expected type or explicit `[...]`, and a call with nothing that fixes `T` is E0301 at the call. The error for a default that does not fit is `TypeError` at the default, never at a call. `[]` is a const expression as a keyword default and a struct field default only, not in a `const` declaration or a range bound; each fill builds a new value. §2.13, §2.14, §2.21. See [rationale](decisions/generic-param-defaults.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 5-1 (AI/ML dissent); Q3-Q4 6-0; AI-First 4/5 (Consistency fail) |
| Qualified names in impl headers | **Qualified name (Q1)**: a module-qualified trait or type name (`lib.Tr`, `lib.Pt`) is legal in every slot that takes the bare name; the spelling never changes scope or the orphan check, which test the resolved trait and type. **Scope (Q2)**: an impl header, bare or qualified, puts no trait in scope; an impl body gets no special treatment, and the repair for a body call is `import lib.{Tr}` or `lib.Tr.name(self)`. **Slots (Q3)**: a general clause, then the list: a bound, a `where` constraint, both names of an impl header, a supertrait clause, the argument of `@derive(...)`, the head of an impl-qualified call; the receiver slot is a type position. **Traits in §10.1 (Q4)**: the list in §10.1 names traits; effects are out of this decision. **Diagnostic (Q5)**: one error for a type where a trait is expected, in every trait slot, bare or qualified; E0740 keeps its number, takes the name `TypeNotTrait`, and absorbs the impl-qualified call head case. §10.1 *Qualified Access*, §3.6 *Impl visibility*, §3c.4 *Impl-Qualified Calls*. See [rationale](decisions/qualified-impl-header.md) | Q1-Q4 6-0; Q5 5-1 (Minimalism dissent); AI-First 5/5 |
| Selective import of an undeclared name | A listed name that the module does not declare is `UnknownImportName` (E1023), checked when the import is read, used or not, with the span on the listed name; `pub import` too; a name declared without `pub` stays E1003. The entry binds a name that denotes no item: no W0602, no E0504/E0507/E1006 at a use, no part in E1005 or E1012. Candidates: `pub` items of the module in both namespaces and its `pub import` names, minus names the list holds; with no near candidate the help lists them (E0547 rule). A case-only match with a private item is still E1023, with a `note:` and no fix. The one-suggestion fix renames the listed name and every use that resolves to the entry (by name resolution; an alias renames the listed name only; a suggestion only when the new name is already bound). §10.5 *Imported Names* says that every check on an import entry runs when the import is read, and each import cause cell repeats it. The qualified miss `m.n` keeps E0505 with the same candidates; moving it is a follow-up | Q1-Q3, Q4 (R2), Q7 (R2) 6-0; Q5 5-1 R2 (Minimalism dissent); Q6 5-1 (Minimalism dissent); AI-First 5/5 |
| Read_line migration help sites | **Sites (Q1)**: the `io.read_line()` migration note and `match` help ride on E0300 (TypeError), E0505 (UnresolvedMethod) on an `Option[Str]` receiver, and E0523 (MissingDisplayImpl) in interpolation; the codes do not change; no fix is machine-applicable. **`+` (Q2)**: not listed; the spec says `+` is not covered by this rule. **E0505 (Q3)**: only when `Str` has a method of that name; the spec lists no method names. **Origin**: a direct call, or a name bound by `let` to it; a copy, a field, a function result and a collection element do not count. **`let mut` (Q4)**: a name the code assigns again does not count; the help does not show when an assignment can reach the use. **Positions (Q5)**: a direct call as the value of a `return` or of a named argument counts. **`.debug()` (Q6)**: on a read_line value the `match` help replaces it; the general `.debug()` bug is not part of this decision. **Golden**: fixes only the note and help lines; the headline and caret label are the compiler's wording for each site. Read_line stays a special case. §4.4.9 *Migration*, ERROR_CATALOG.md. See [rationale](decisions/read-line-help-sites.md) | Q1-Q3, Q5, Q6 6-0; Q4 5-1, Minimalism dissent |
| Effects of a derived operation body | **Rule (Q1)**: a derived operation body, written in the effect declaration, may perform only the leaf sub-effect it is declared in; an operation declaration has no `!` row; a built-in effect (`IO.Log`), another user effect and a sibling sub-effect under the same root are refused the same way; nothing is charged to the handler or the `with`; the clause row does not change. **Diagnostic**: new `DerivedOpForeignEffect` E0557, not E0500; the help is prose with no `!` list and no `fix:` line. **Help (Q2)**: two fixes in this order, a plain function with its own `!` row, then a primitive operation implemented in each handler; a sibling gets one more line saying it is a different effect and may have no handler. §4.7.1, catalog E0557. See [rationale](decisions/derived-op-effects.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 5-1 (Systems dissent: a third help line); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Main file is an ordinary module | **Q1**: the file that declares `fn main` is an ordinary module; its name comes from its path under `src/` or `@module`, and the file given to `blink build`/`blink run` keeps it; a sibling imports it selectively or by qualifier, and there `main` binds only the qualifier (E1012 for a sibling with its own `fn main`; E1006 keeps its qualified-access help). **Q2**: the §10.1 tree shows `src/myapp.bl` as the package entry. **Q3**: a file outside `src/` has no module name and no file can import it; E1200 gets the help "files outside `src/` are not modules; move the file under `src/`". **Q4**: a file is one module however the build reaches it. **Q5**: no sentence on `main.x` inside that file; scope rules decide it. §10.1. See [rationale](decisions/main-file-module.md) | Q1-Q3 6-0; Q4 5-1 (DevOps dissent); Q5 6-0 R2 after 4-2 R1; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Supertrait check under the impl's own bounds | An impl of a trait with supertraits proves each supertrait for its receiver type using only the bounds it writes (binder or `where` clause) and their supertraits; else E0911 `MissingSupertraitImpl` at the impl header, never at a use site; no implied bounds; a derived supertrait impl counts, with a label on the `@derive` attribute. **Span (Q2)**: primary span on the binder parameter that lacks the bound, the headline names the impl and the supertrait, else the trait reference. **Rollout (Q3)**: hard error at once. **Q4**: the `Eq` that `@derive(Ord)` or `@derive(Hash)` adds carries the bounds of `@derive(Eq)`. **Help (Q5)**: machine-applicable, adds only the bounds not already entailed, in the impl's existing form. §3.6. See [rationale](decisions/supertrait-impl-bounds.md) | Q1, Q3, Q4 6-0; Q2 6-0 R2 (was 4-1-1); Q5 5-1 (Minimalism dissent); AI-First 5/5 |
| Type parameters on effect operations | **Rule (Q1)**: in v1 an effect operation, primitive or derived, takes no type parameters, and an effect takes none (`effect Store[T]`); the parser reads the `[...]` after the name and reports one new code for both shapes, `EffectOpTypeParams` E0558, not `expected (, got [`. The generic surface is a plain function that calls a concrete operation, as `io.println` does (§4.4). A derived body may still call a generic function and use instantiated types. This is its own deferral, separate from the effect-kinded `Handler[E]` deferral ("Handler generic parameters"), with a cross-reference and no dependency. A later version can relax the rule without breaking a program; the code is then retired. **Note (Q2, Q3)**: the golden diagnostic has one `note:` in language terms, "a handler provides each operation of an effect with one fixed signature", and no lowering word in sections/. **Help (Q4)**: one `help:` with both parts, concrete types and a generic wrapper that uses `T: Display` and `x.display()`; the help shows an instantiated type such as `List[Str]` as concrete. **Spec (Q5)**: sections/ adds one sentence, "A later version may allow type parameters on an operation." §4.12, catalog E0558. See [rationale](decisions/effect-op-type-params.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 5-1 (Minimalism dissent); Q3 4-2 R2 after a five-way split R1 (PLT, DevOps dissent); Q4 5-1 (DevOps dissent); Q5 4-2 R2 after 3-3 R1 (Systems, Minimalism dissent); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Annotation arguments that do not match a row's form | **Rule (Option 1, 6-0)**: §11.1 rule 4. Arguments that do not match the form the row's Annotation column writes are one family error, never a warning, never accepted: arguments on a row that takes none, no arguments on a row that needs them, the wrong count or kind, an unknown key. The parser reads any argument list in one general form and one check compares it with the row, before any name or scope lookup. One diagnostic per annotation; a row's own code wins for the exact failure it names (bare `@trusted` stays E0836, `@derive(Foo)` stays E1402, E0842, E0847, E0913); an unknown key is the family error. The annotation is invalid, its claim is not checked, the rest of the declaration is. **Code (Q1)**: `AnnotationArguments` (E1134). **Span (Q2)**: the bad argument; the argument list when the count is wrong; the annotation name when no list exists. **Tests (Q3)**: the ci table check needs, for each row that can fail, one negative test per mismatch kind that row can have, and lists the missing (row, kind) pairs; the check verifies that each test fails with `AnnotationArguments` for its stated kind; this is a requirement on the check, not spec text. **`@deprecated` (Q4)**: the row says key order is free and keeps the optional marks next to the form; the required keys do not change. Help prints the row's form; a near-miss key gets "did you mean"; LSP offers "remove arguments" only on a row that takes none; `blink fmt` leaves the annotation as written. §11.1, ERROR_CATALOG. See [rationale](decisions/annotation-arguments.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 6-0; Q3 5-1 R2 after 3-2-1 R1 (Minimalism dissent); Q4 5-1 (Minimalism dissent); AI-First 5/5 pass |
| Reserved built-in effect names | **Rule (Q1)**: the built-in effect names `FS`, `Net`, `DB`, `IO`, `Env`, `Time`, `Rand`, `Crypto`, `Process`, `State` and `Async` are reserved; a top-level user `effect` with one of them is `ReservedEffectName` (E0559) in every module, `pub` or not. So `Time` always means the built-in in rows, `with`, handlers and `time.x()`, with no import; a per-module shadow with W1010 is rejected. A sub-effect with the name stays legal (`effect Clock { effect Time { ... } }`). The §10.6 *Shadowing Rules* `effect` clause does not cover these names; the §10.6 table stays types-only and the shadow ruling for type names is unchanged. §4.3 holds the list and gains `Async`. **Type (Q2)**: a built-in effect name reserves the `effect` declaration only; `type Time` is legal, and in a type position the user type is the one named in its module. **Definition line (Q3)**: none; §4.3 stays the one list (owner tiebreak). **Help**: rename, and `handler Time { ... }` with `with` to replace the clock in tests. The LSP rename fix is tooling. §4.3, §4.12, §10.6, §3b, catalog E0559. See [rationale](decisions/reserved-effect-names.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 5-1 (Minimalism dissent: the fact only, no resolution clause); Q3 3-3, owner broke the tie for B; AI-First 5/5 pass |
| The root package (gate for declaration-editing help) | **Q1**: the root package is the package of the nearest `blink.toml` above the file the tool started from, with its `src/`, `tests/`, `examples/` and `bench/`; path, git and std dependencies do not count; no workspace concept; the CLI and the LSP print the same text. **Q2**: for a type outside the root package the spec adds no line; only the use-site help shows. **Q3**: §2.14, E0315 and E0525 say "root package" in place of "the package being compiled". **Q4**: no §3.1 pointer; the gate is stated in §10.5 only. E0505 reachability, the orphan rule, E0518 and E1016 keep their text. §10.5 *Project root discovery*, §2.14. See [rationale](decisions/root-package.md) | Q1 6-0; Q2 5-1 (DevOps dissent); Q3 6-0; Q4 5-1 (PLT dissent); AI-First 5/5 |
| Fields of a paren variant and a mixed payload | **Declaration (Q1)**: a paren variant names every field or none; `M(Int, b: Int)` and `M(a: Int, Int)` are `MixedVariantFields` (E0320) at the variant, help "name every field or none". **Payload order (Q3)**: positional arguments come first; a positional argument after a label is `PositionalAfterKeyword` (E0527), the §2.13 code widened from calls to variant paren payloads. **Fill (agreed)**: argument *i* fills field *i*, a label fills the field it names, then `DuplicateField`, `NoSuchField`, `MissingField` run on that fill, in that order; `Circle(1.0, radius: 2.0)` is a `DuplicateField` and a `MissingField`, no longer a build-time ICE. A call may write any field by position or, if the variant names it, by label (Q6); `Circle(1.0, 2)` and `Circle(1.5, tag: 2)` stay legal. **E0300 (agreed)**: only for a payload with no label, or with more positional arguments than fields. **Note (Q4, Q5)**: the fields note marks a field that an argument filled, by position or by label, with one mark, `(given)`, and prints on the E0300 count error and on `MissingField`, not on `DuplicateField`, whose message names the filling argument. **Unnamed field (Q2)**: moot once Q1 forbids the mix; had the mix stayed, `M(b: 1)` was `MissingField` for field 0 with help to write it by position. A user who wrote a mixed declaration gets a hard error with no deprecation step | Q1 6-0, Q2 6-0, Q3 6-0, Q4 5-1 (Minimalism dissent: no mark), Q5 6-0, Q6 6-0; AI-First 5/5 |

---

## Open Questions

These design decisions remain unresolved:

1. **Information flow tracking** — Taint tracking via effect provenance (v2+ roadmap). `Template[C]` covers injection cases for v1
2. ~~**Row polymorphism**~~ — **Partially resolved.** Wildcard `! _` forwarding covers 95% case in v1. Named effect variables (full row polymorphism) deferred to v2. See [Effect Polymorphism rationale](decisions/effect-polymorphism.md).
3. **Higher-kinded types** — Only if needed for effect abstractions. Deferred.
4. ~~**Codegen backend**~~ — **Resolved.** Emit C → cc. See [Codegen Backend & Bootstrap rationale](decisions/codegen-backend-bootstrap.md).
